VibeRounds Clinical Intelligence Suite

A deterministic compute / LLM-interpret / human-gate analytics platform for clinical cohort research — re-platformed from the original PoC into a real monorepo.

100+ analytics
3 disease modules
any LLM
100k–1M+ patient cohorts
FHIR R4 ready
Core Principle how every analytic flows
1 · Deterministic computestatistics from cohort data, pure functions
→
2 · LLM interpretationdrafts hypotheses only — never sees case-level data
→
3 · Human gatea person approves or rejects every draft
Architecture monorepo layout

packages/core

Shared domain types, stat utilities (mean, median, Kaplan–Meier, percentile rank), the deterministic synthetic cohort generator, the data-quality gate, and the v3 event-stream model.

packages/analytics · -v2 · -v3

Three analytic registries: v1 (text-based, 113), v2 (terminology-coded, 119), v3 (longitudinal event-stream, 49). Each analytic is a pure cases → result module registered by id.

apps/api

Fastify server: /cohort, /analytics, /analytics/:id/run, in-memory audit log, version routing, and server-side FHIR fetching.

apps/web

React + Vite shell with sidebar, data-source controls, quality badge, cohort builder, Suite, Patient Data Explorer, and analytics pages. Charts via shared LineChart / HBars components.

Version History v1 → v2 → v3 — how data modeling evolves

v1 · Text-based analytics (built & working)

How it approaches data: everything is plain text strings — conditions, medications, and symptoms are labels like "fever" or "ACEi/ARB". FHIR ingestion extracts only display text and discards the codes; the database stores text arrays.

How it works: analytics are pure JavaScript modules matching on exact string equality. The synthetic generator produces text labels only.

Limitations: no hierarchy awareness, no synonyms ("fever" ≠ "pyrexia"), brittle across naming variations, and each case is a flat snapshot with no sense of when things happened.

v2 · Terminology-aware analytics (built & working)

How it approaches data: text labels plus coded versions — SNOMED CT, LOINC, RxNorm — stored side by side in the same Case. Coded fields also carry dates (onset, recorded, medication start/end), and the database adds JSONB columns for the coded fields. The FHIR loader preserves full Coding objects instead of discarding them.

How it works: a terminology module defines standard bindings and a hierarchy module supports descendant traversal; v2 analytics group by code and query "all cardiovascular conditions" by hierarchy rather than hardcoded lists. v1 analytics keep running untouched, and the API routes by Accept-Version header — v1 default, v2 opt-in.

What it unlocks: synonym resolution ("MI" = "heart attack"), cross-source consistency between EHRs, automatic coverage of new SNOMED concepts, and dates on clinical facts — the raw material v3 builds on.

v3 · Longitudinal event stream (built & working)

How it approaches data: the atom is the PatientEvent — one dated clinical fact for one patient (encounter, diagnosis, comorbidity, medication, lab, procedure, symptom, outcome). A patient's event stream sorted by time becomes the primary record; episodes, observation periods, and exposure windows are derived from it. No new loaders: toEvents() flattens the existing v2 Case, whose coded arrays have carried dates since v2.

How it works — temporality is the point: every event sits at its own date, never collapsed onto the admit date (which is only a fallback when the data has none). Outcome events are dated at discharge — the moment they became known — so sequencing is honest. Analytics run over real dates: first-observed ordering for co-occurrence vs. sequence ("did the symptom precede the drug, or follow it?"), medication exposure windows (start/end/status), KM over time-to-event, gap analysis, seasonal patterns, and an implicit "as of" clock at the latest event date. Analytic ids stay compatible with the v1 catalog, and the API serves the v3 longitudinal catalog via Accept-Version: 3.

Honesty by design: outcome events ("escalation", "readmission-90d") are modeled deterministically at import — the audit carries a systemic caveat that stratifying them by age / comorbidity / polypharmacy is circular, so every temporal result is read with that in mind.

Analytics Catalog every analytic button on the Suite page, per version

The Suite page renders one button bar per category. Disease-module analytics are not shown here — they live under the Disease sidebar group (selected via the toolbar dropdown) rather than the Suite page. Page outputs are the exception: PICO/PECO Study (v1–v3), Patient data explorer (v1–v3) and Knowledge graph (v2–v3) render as Suite buttons that open inline in the results flow. Analytics missing from the active engine version render as disabled buttons with a version hint (e.g. “Pathway discovery · V3”) instead of disappearing, so no category group is ever empty.

Current Suite behavior version gating, inline pages, persistence, pagination
  • Version-aware buttons. Every category group always renders its full button set. An analytic missing from the active engine version shows as a disabled button with a version hint — “Requires engine V3” for the longitudinal event-stream analytics (process mining, medication sequences, journey map), “V2 only” for coded-terminology variants — so switching V1/V2/V3 never hides or empties a group.
  • Inline page outputs. PICO/PECO Study (v1–v3), Patient data explorer (v1–v3) and Knowledge graph (v2–v3) are not navigation links: clicking them appends the full interactive page as a block in the results flow, like every analytic output. Each block has its own ✕ remove button; “Re-run suite” skips page blocks; save/load round-trips them.
  • In-place updates. Interactive outputs (e.g. the Patient journey map’s “Show journey”, “Add to map”, “Plot full cohort”) re-run their analytic and replace their own block’s result in place — same slot, same order — instead of freezing or appending duplicates.
  • Reload persistence. The active page and the entire suite flow (analytics and page blocks) are persisted to localStorage and restored on reload, size-guarded at 4 MB so oversized results never break saving.
  • Pagination everywhere it matters. Every table that can grow with cohort size (case lists, itemsets, pathways, signals, rules) paginates with 10/25/50/100 rows per page and self-clamping pages; bar charts cap at 30 entries with the full long tail in the paginated table.
  • No raw JSON dumps. A smart fallback renderer turns any analytic result into a summary line plus a paginated table of its first tabular array (rows/signals/groups/plots…) — only truly non-tabular results show formatted JSON.

Source Graph who imports what — drawn from the real imports in the codebase

Every arrow below is an actual import found in the source (182 files, 277 import edges, aggregated for readability). Read it top-down: the apps sit on the registries, the registries sit on one shared core, and the core's ten modules each do one job. Nothing points upward — analytics never call the server, and the core never calls the analytics. That one-way flow is what keeps every analytic a pure, testable function.

  • One-way flow. Apps import registries, registries import core, core imports nothing of theirs. No cycles exist in the graph — which is exactly what lets every analytic be tested in isolation.
  • The barrel. All three registries reach the core through a single entry point (index.ts re-exports all ten modules), so adding a core capability is a one-line change visible to every version.
  • Version drift is visible. v1 and v2 lean on the same stat helpers (62 and 71 import edges into core respectively); v3 touches the core far more narrowly (7 edges) — almost everything routes through the event model instead. The graph makes the architectural shift literal.
  • Where to look when something breaks. A wrong number in an analytic traces down: analytic → registry package → core module → the pure function. Each hop is one file.
Data Flow & Server Internals where a number actually comes from — runtime path and the api module map

The Source Graph shows who imports whom. This section shows the runtime path: how data enters the system, where it rests, who computes on it, and where the LLM and the human gate sit. Every route and module named below is real — taken from apps/api/src/server.ts and the modules it loads. (The analytics themselves are deliberately single nodes here — each one's full code already lives in the Under the Hood section.)

A · End-to-end data flow

B · Inside apps/api — the module map

  • Two storage modes, one code path. Small cohorts live in memory; Database mode bulk-loads PostgreSQL and analytics flip to SQL aggregation — with the same result shapes, so renderers don't change. Analytics that can't run as SQL report themselves skipped rather than silently degrading.
  • The LLM is a side branch, not a stage. It receives only computed result summaries, never case-level data, and everything it drafts lands in the human gate as pending. Unplug it and every deterministic number is unchanged.
  • The audit log is a cross-cutting sink, not a step: runs, FHIR queries, LLM calls and gate decisions all land in it regardless of which path produced them.
  • Dashed boxes are outside the process — FHIR servers, uploaded files, your own database, the LLM providers. Everything solid is code you can open and read.
Under the Hood code of every Create-Suite button — v1, v2, v3, in plain English

Each entry: a plain-English summary, then the verbatim registered source code. Read it as a method section. One implementation, three consistent versions: the Visual analytics and Statistics entries in V2 are thin wrappers — they convert the Case cohort into the same PatientEvent stream via core's toEvents() and delegate to the identical V3 implementation. That guarantees identical numbers for the same cohort in any version, one place to fix the math (Cox, FP-Growth…), and — crucially — each version keeps its own quality gate: a V2 wrapper grades on V2 fields (e.g. lab-box-plots grades on observationCodes coverage), so on a text-only cohort it honestly reports weak instead of pretending.

V1 — 44 Suite buttons
Cohort overview by age band

The smallest analytic in the registry — it exists to prove the whole pipeline works. Its full source is below.

 {
  id: "age-bands",
  name: "Cohort overview by age band",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "Headcount, share of cohort, median length of stay, and 90-day readmission rate per age band. Trivial by design — it is the end-to-end plumbing test.",
  requiredFields: ["age", "losDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const rows: AgeBandRow[] = BANDS.map((band) => {
      const members = cases.filter((c) => bandFor(c.age) === band);
      const los = members.map((m) => m.losDays);
      const readmits = members.filter((m) => m.readmitted90d).length;
      const medLos = los.length
        ? [...los].sort((a, b) => a - b)[Math.floor(los.length / 2)]
        : 0;
      return {
        band,
        count: members.length,
        pct: cases.length ? members.length / cases.length : 0,
        medianLos: medLos,
        readmitPct: members.length ? readmits / members.length : 0,
        caseIds: members.map((m) => m.id),
      };
    });
    void groupBy; // groupBy stays available for richer banding later
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's age, losDays, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Bands patients into age groups (45–54, 55–64, 65–74, 75–84, 85+), then counts cases, share of cohort, median LOS, and readmission rate within each band.",
    output: "rows: one per age band with {band, count, pct, medianLos, readmitPct} — a demographic baseline, not a claim of causation.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "readmitted90d is a modeled generator output (rand()<0.22 in synthetic.ts; formula in fhir.ts), so readmitPct per band is a formula restated.",
      "Age is an input to the outcome formulas (escProb adds (age>65)*0.08; fhir.ts ties vaccinated to age>65) — the age gradient is injected, not discovered.",
      "losDays is randInt(1,21) — medianLos per band describes the generator's dice, not throughput.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "readmitPct by age band is circular: age feeds the modeled readmission/escalation formulas.",
    ],
  },
};
Data quality assessment (v1)

Checks how trustworthy the loaded cohort is: for every field family the analytics rely on, it measures the share of patients that actually have data and grades it good / limited / weak, plus cohort-level category diversity and clinical-content presence. This is the quality check every other result should be read against.

 {
  id: "data-quality",
  name: "Data quality assessment (v1)",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "Grades the cohort's fitness for V1 string-based analytics: field coverage across demographics, clinical content, outcomes, and utilization. Every V1 analytic reads these text fields, so coverage here bounds what the engine can match.",
  requiredFields: ["id"],
  run(cases) {
    const total = cases.length;
    const checks: Check[] = [];

    // ── field coverage ──
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Patient id", cases.filter((c) => c.id).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Age", cases.filter((c) => typeof c.age === "number").length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Sex", cases.filter((c) => c.sex).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Category (condition family)", cases.filter((c) => c.category).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Primary diagnosis", cases.filter((c) => c.diagnosis).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Comorbidities (list)", cases.filter((c) => c.comorbidities?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Medications (list)", cases.filter((c) => c.medications?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Symptoms (list)", cases.filter((c) => c.symptoms?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Investigations (list)", cases.filter((c) => c.investigations?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Procedures (list)", cases.filter((c) => c.procedures?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Admit date", cases.filter((c) => c.admitDate).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Length of stay", cases.filter((c) => typeof c.losDays === "number").length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Disposition", cases.filter((c) => c.disposition).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("90-day readmission flag", cases.filter((c) => c.readmitted90d !== undefined && c.readmitted90d !== null).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Time-to-escalation", cases.filter((c) => c.timeToEscalationDays !== null && c.timeToEscalationDays !== undefined).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Polypharmacy count", cases.filter((c) => typeof c.polypharmacyCount === "number").length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Checkpoints (pathway)", cases.filter((c) => c.checkpoints?.length).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("SDOH barriers", cases.filter((c) => c.sdohBarriers !== undefined).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Insurance", cases.filter((c) => c.insurance).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Region", cases.filter((c) => c.region).length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("90-day encounter count", cases.filter((c) => typeof c.encounters90d === "number").length, total));
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Admit month", cases.filter((c) => typeof c.admitMonth === "number").length, total));

    // ── coded fields (optional in v1, but they power the richer analytics
    //    when present — graded informationally) ──
    const codedInfo = (label: string, present: number) => {
      const pct = total ? Math.round((present / total) * 100) : 0;
      checks.push({
        check: `${label} (coded — optional in v1)`,
        status: pct >= 50 ? "good" : pct >= 20 ? "limited" : "weak",
        detail: `${present}/${total} cases (${pct}%) — enables coded matching in v2/v3`,
      });
    };
    codedInfo("Diagnosis codes", cases.filter((c) => (c.diagnosisCodes ?? []).some((cd) => cd.code)).length);
    codedInfo("Comorbidity codes", cases.filter((c) => (c.comorbidityCodes ?? []).some((cd) => cd.code)).length);
    codedInfo("Medication codes", cases.filter((c) => (c.medicationCodes ?? []).some((cd) => cd.code)).length);
    checks.push(coverageCheck(
      "Numeric lab values (powers box/violin plots)",
      cases.filter((c) => (c.observationCodes ?? []).some((o) => o.value !== undefined && o.value !== null)).length,
      total,
    ));

    // ── cohort-level checks ──
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    checks.push({
      check: "Distinct categories",
      status: byCat.size >= 5 ? "good" : byCat.size >= 2 ? "limited" : "weak",
      detail: `${byCat.size} categories`,
    });
    const withAnyProblem = cases.filter((c) => c.diagnosis || c.comorbidities?.length).length;
    checks.push(coverageCheck("Any clinical content (diagnosis or comorbidity)", withAnyProblem, total));

    const weak = checks.filter((c) => c.status === "weak").length;
    const limited = checks.filter((c) => c.status === "limited").length;
    const overall: Status = weak > 0 ? "weak" : limited > 2 ? "limited" : "good";

    return {
      version: "v1 — string matching",
      overall,
      summary: `${checks.length - weak - limited}/${checks.length} checks good; ${limited} limited; ${weak} weak`,
      rows: checks.map((c) => ({ check: c.check, status: c.status, detail: c.detail })),
    };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The whole cohort (v1 Case snapshot).",
    process: "For each field family the v1 engine reads, computes the share of cases with the field populated, and grades good (≥50%), limited (20–50%), or weak (<20%). Cohort-level category diversity and clinical-content presence are also checked.",
    output: "version, overall grade, summary line, and rows: one {check, status, detail} per coverage check — read the weak rows as the cohort's blind spots for string matching.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Coverage measures population, not correctness — a fully-populated modeled field is still generator output.",
      "Thresholds (20%/50%) are heuristics; a rare-but-important field can be clinically vital below them.",
    ],
  },
};
Backward lift

For each condition category it checks every other condition patients have and asks: do patients who ALSO have this condition get worse more often? It compares the get-worse rate with the condition versus without it, and reports whichever condition makes the biggest difference.

 {
  id: "backward-lift",
  name: "Backward lift",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Per category, scans every comorbidity and reports the one whose presence lifts the escalation rate the most. Pure 2×2 arithmetic — deterministic code only.",
  requiredFields: ["category", "comorbidities", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    const rows: BackwardLiftRow[] = [];
    byCat.forEach((group, cat) => {
      let best: BackwardLiftRow | null = null;
      for (const com of ALL_COMORBIDITIES) {
        if (com === cat) continue;
        const withCom = group.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.includes(com));
        const withoutCom = group.filter((c) => !c.comorbidities.includes(com));
        if (withCom.length < MIN_WITH || withoutCom.length < MIN_WITHOUT) continue;
        const a = withCom.filter(escalated).length;
        const b = withoutCom.filter(escalated).length;
        const rWith = a / withCom.length;
        const rWithout = b / withoutCom.length;
        const lift =
          rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : rWith > 0 ? LIFT_CAP : 0;
        if (!best || lift > best.lift) {
          best = {
            category: cat,
            comorbidity: com,
            a,
            b,
            c: withCom.length - a,
            d: withoutCom.length - b,
            n: withCom.length,
            rWith,
            rWithout,
            lift,
            caseIds: withCom.map((c) => c.id),
          };
        }
      }
      if (best) rows.push(best);
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((x, y) => y.lift - x.lift) };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withEsc = cases.filter(escalated).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withEsc / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) {
      return {
        level: "weak",
        gaps: [`escalation outcome present in only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort`],
      };
    }
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's category, comorbidities, and escalation outcome (timeToEscalationDays).",
    process: "Within each category, scans every comorbidity with at least 4 patients having it and 2 without, computes escalation rate with vs without, and keeps the comorbidity with the highest lift (with ÷ without).",
    output: "rows: one per category with {category, comorbidity, a, b, c, d, n, rWith, rWithout, lift} — the full 2×2 is returned so every lift is auditable.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "comorbidities drive escalation in the generator (synthetic.ts plants 90% escalation for the INJECTED_LIFT condition) — the top lift is often literally planted.",
      "Scanning ALL_COMORBIDITIES per category and keeping the max is a winner's curse: no multiplicity penalty, extreme lifts win.",
      "MIN_WITH=4 / MIN_WITHOUT=2 let four-patient cells post lift 99 via the LIFT_CAP sentinel.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Comorbidity is an input to the modeled escalation formula; the scan-max then overstates the planted effect.",
    ],
  },
};
Backward factors

Same with-versus-without comparison as Backward lift, but for five fixed patient factors (social barriers, many medications, age, number of conditions, screening status): split the patients and report how much more often the 'has it' group gets worse.

 {
  id: "backward-factors",
  name: "Backward factors",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Same with/without escalation lift arithmetic as Backward lift, applied cohort-wide to a fixed set of coded factors (SDOH, polypharmacy, age, comorbidity burden, screening status).",
  requiredFields: ["sdohBarriers", "polypharmacyCount", "age", "comorbidities", "screeningUpToDate"],
  run(cases) {
    const rows: FactorRow[] = FACTORS.map((f) => {
      const withF = cases.filter(f.test);
      const withoutF = cases.filter((c) => !f.test(c));
      const rWith = withF.length ? withF.filter(escalated).length / withF.length : 0;
      const rWithout = withoutF.length ? withoutF.filter(escalated).length / withoutF.length : 0;
      const lift = rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : rWith > 0 ? 99 : 0;
      return { label: f.label, n: withF.length, nBase: withoutF.length, rWith, rWithout, lift, caseIds: withF.map((c) => c.id) };
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.lift - a.lift) };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's sdohBarriers, polypharmacyCount, age, comorbidities, and screeningUpToDate, plus escalation outcome.",
    process: "For each of five fixed factors, splits the cohort into patients with and without it, computes the escalation rate in each arm, and reports lift = rateWith ÷ rateWithout (capped at 99 when the without-rate is zero).",
    output: "rows: one per factor with {label, n, nBase, rWith, rWithout, lift}, sorted worst lift first — read lift as how many times higher escalation is with the factor.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Four of five factors (SDOH, 5+ meds, age 65+, 3+ comorbidities) are verbatim terms of the modeled escalation formula — lift restates the generator.",
      "screeningUpToDate is itself a modeled flag correlated with the same inputs — no factor is an independent exposure.",
      "Univariate with/without arms: age, polypharmacy and comorbidities co-occur, so each lift is confounded by the others.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Every factor is an input to the modeled escalation outcome — lift is circular, not evidence.",
    ],
  },
};
Forward hypothesis

You name a condition category and a second condition; it finds the patients who have both and compares their outcomes (getting worse, coming back, stay length, medication count) to similar patients who don't have the second condition.

 {
  id: "forward-hypothesis",
  name: "Forward hypothesis",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Tests one stated hypothesis — a category plus a comorbidity you name — and reads off escalation, readmission, length-of-stay, and medication counts against the same-category baseline.",
  requiredFields: ["category", "comorbidities", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases, params) {
    const cat = params?.cat;
    const com = params?.com;
    if (!cat || !com) {
      return { error: "params 'cat' (category) and 'com' (comorbidity) are required" };
    }
    const cohort = cases.filter((c) => c.category === cat && c.comorbidities.includes(com));
    const baseline = cases.filter((c) => c.category === cat && !c.comorbidities.includes(com));
    const result: ForwardPairResult = {
      cat,
      com,
      n: cohort.length,
      nBase: baseline.length,
      escRate: rate(cohort, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
      escRateBase: rate(baseline, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
      readmit: rate(cohort, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
      readmitBase: rate(baseline, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
      los: rate(cohort, (c) => c.losDays),
      losBase: rate(baseline, (c) => c.losDays),
      meds: rate(cohort, (c) => c.polypharmacyCount),
      medsBase: rate(baseline, (c) => c.polypharmacyCount),
      caseIds: cohort.map((c) => c.id)
    };
    return { pair: result };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const escPct = cases.length ? cases.filter(escalated).length / cases.length : 0;
    return escPct < 0.2
      ? { level: "weak", gaps: ["escalation outcome sparse in cohort"] }
      : { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Params cat (category) and com (comorbidity); each case's category, comorbidities, readmitted90d, losDays, polypharmacyCount, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Selects patients in the named category who carry the named comorbidity, and reads off escalation, readmission, LOS, and medication counts against the same-category patients without it.",
    output: "pair: {cat, com, n, nBase, escRate, escRateBase, readmit, readmitBase, los, losBase, meds, medsBase} — a direct hypothesis test, no scanning or ranking.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "comorbidities drive escalation, readmission and polypharmacy in the generator — a 'confirmed' hypothesis restates escProb.",
      "The same-category baseline is contaminated: baseline patients carry other comorbidities with their own injected lifts.",
      "No minimum n — a two-patient hypothesis can return a finite-looking rate contrast.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The tested exposure is an input to the modeled outcomes — confirmation is circular.",
    ],
  },
};
Closed loop

Runs the discovery process on one batch of patients, keeps the strongest findings, then checks each finding again on a completely fresh batch. A finding only counts if the same pattern shows up again — like re-running an experiment to confirm it.

 {
  id: "closed-loop",
  name: "Closed loop",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Takes the strongest backward-lift discoveries (lift ≥ 1.35) and attempts to replicate each on a freshly generated cohort it was not discovered on. A finding counts only if the lift replicates.",
  requiredFields: ["category", "comorbidities", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases, params) {
    const seed = Number(params?.seed ?? 42);
    void cases; // discovery regenerates from the seed so the loop is self-contained
    const discoveryCohort = buildCohort(seed, 500);
    const discovery = (
      backwardLift.run(discoveryCohort.cases).rows as Array<{
        category: string;
        comorbidity: string;
        n: number;
        lift: number;
      }>
    ).filter((r) => r.lift >= DISCOVERY_LIFT_MIN && Number.isFinite(r.lift));

    const valCohort = buildCohort(seed + VALIDATION_SEED_OFFSET, 500);
    const byCat = groupBy(valCohort.cases, (c) => c.category);

    const rows: ClosedLoopRow[] = discovery.map((d) => {
      const group = byCat.get(d.category) ?? [];
      const withCom = group.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.includes(d.comorbidity));
      const withoutCom = group.filter((c) => !c.comorbidities.includes(d.comorbidity));
      const rWith = withCom.length ? withCom.filter(escalated).length / withCom.length : 0;
      const rWithout = withoutCom.length ? withoutCom.filter(escalated).length / withoutCom.length : 0;
      const valLift =
        rWithout > 0 && withCom.length >= 3 ? rWith / rWithout : null;
      return {
        ...d,
        valN: withCom.length,
        valLift,
        pass: valLift !== null && valLift >= VALIDATION_LIFT_MIN,
        caseIds: withCom.map((c) => c.id),
      };
    });
    return { rows, valSeed: valCohort.meta.seed };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "A run seed param (default 42); the loaded cohort itself is not used.",
    process: "Generates a 500-case synthetic discovery cohort from the seed, runs backward lift, keeps discoveries with lift ≥ 1.35, then re-tests each on a fresh synthetic cohort from seed + 9973; a finding passes only if its lift replicates ≥ 1.3.",
    output: "rows: one discovery with {category, comorbidity, n, lift, valN, valLift, pass}, plus valSeed — note the data is modeled by the synthetic generator, not your loaded cases.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Both discovery and validation cohorts come from buildCohort(seed) — the loaded cohort is ignored entirely (cases is voided).",
      "Replication re-tests the same deterministic generator: findings that encode escProb's structure replicate; real-world signals would not.",
      "500-case arms with a hard pass threshold (lift ≥ 1.3) and no CI — pass/fail flips on seed choice.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Validation uses a second sample from the same generator — replication here cannot validate against reality.",
    ],
  },
};
Co-occurrence

Finds pairs of conditions that show up in the same patient, reports how often patients with each pair get worse, and guesses which condition 'came first' by alphabetical order (a simple stand-in for real timing).

 {
  id: "cooccurrence",
  name: "Co-occurrence",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Every comorbidity pair that co-occurs in a patient, with the shared-escalation rate and, where both escalated, which condition's escalation came first more often.",
  requiredFields: ["comorbidities", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const pairs = new Map<string, PairRec>();
    const bump = (a: string, b: string, c: Case) => {
      const key = a < b ? `${a} + ${b}` : `${b} + ${a}`;
      let rec = pairs.get(key);
      if (!rec) {
        rec = { a: a < b ? a : b, b: a < b ? b : a, n: 0, bothEsc: 0, aLeads: 0, caseIds: [] };
        pairs.set(key, rec);
      }
      rec.n++;
      rec.caseIds.push(c.id);
      if (escalated(c) && c.timeToEscalationDays !== null) {
        rec.bothEsc++;
        // single time-to-escalation per case: attribute the lead to the
        // alphabetically-first condition deterministically, as sequencing
        // proxy — the PoC used per-node days, which our schema doesn't carry.
        rec.aLeads++;
      }
    };
    for (const c of cases) {
      for (let i = 0; i < c.comorbidities.length; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < c.comorbidities.length; j++) {
          bump(c.comorbidities[i], c.comorbidities[j], c);
        }
      }
    }
    const rows = [...pairs.values()]
      .map((r) => ({
        a: r.a,
        b: r.b,
        n: r.n,
        bothEscRate: r.n ? r.bothEsc / r.n : 0,
        sequence:
          r.bothEsc > 0
            ? `both escalated in ${r.bothEsc} cases`
            : "no shared escalations",
        caseIds: r.caseIds,
      }))
      .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n)
      .slice(0, 10);
    void groupBy;
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withTwo = cases.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.length >= 2).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withTwo / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`multi-comorbidity cases: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    if (pct < 0.5) return { level: "limited", gaps: [`multi-comorbidity cases: only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's comorbidities and escalation outcome (timeToEscalationDays).",
    process: "Tallies every comorbidity pair co-occurring within a patient, computes the shared-escalation rate per pair, and attributes the escalation lead to the alphabetically-first condition as a deterministic sequencing proxy.",
    output: "rows: top 10 pairs with {a, b, n, bothEscRate, sequence} — sequence is a proxy, not a true order of events.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "bothEscRate is circular: comorbidities are inputs to the modeled escalation outcome (INJECTED_LIFT in synthetic.ts).",
      "aLeads increments for the alphabetically-first condition on every shared escalation — sequencing is a fixed artifact, not an observed order.",
      "Multi-coded cases contribute one row per pair — pair counts share patients, so n overstates precision.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Pair escalation rates restate the generator's comorbidity→escalation formula.",
    ],
  },
};
Phenotype clusters

Groups patients by which combination of conditions they have (not by their main category), using a clustering method with no randomness — the same patients always end up in the same groups.

 {
  id: "phenotype-clusters",
  name: "Phenotype clusters",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Deterministic k-means over comorbidity profiles: groups patients by their co-occurring bundle of conditions rather than their primary category. Seeded without randomness, so the same cohort always produces the same clusters.",
  requiredFields: ["comorbidities"],
  run(cases, params) {
    const k = Math.min(Math.max(Number(params?.k ?? 4), 2), 6);
    const pool = cases.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.length > 0);
    if (pool.length < k * 6) {
      return { k, n: pool.length, clusters: [], ready: false };
    }
    const vecOf = (c: Case): number[] => ALL_COMORBIDITIES.map((name) => (c.comorbidities.includes(name) ? 1 : 0));
    const vectors = pool.map(vecOf);
    const dist2 = (a: number[], b: number[]) => a.reduce((s, v, i) => s + (v - b[i]) * (v - b[i]), 0);

    const order = [...pool.keys()].sort(
      (a, b) => pool[a].comorbidities.length - pool[b].comorbidities.length || pool[a].id.localeCompare(pool[b].id)
    );
    let centroids = Array.from({ length: k }, (_, i) => vectors[order[Math.floor((i * order.length) / k)]].slice());
    let assign = new Array(vectors.length).fill(0);

    for (let iter = 0; iter < 30; iter++) {
      let changed = false;
      vectors.forEach((v, i) => {
        let best = 0;
        let bestD = Infinity;
        centroids.forEach((c, ci) => {
          const d = dist2(v, c);
          if (d < bestD) {
            bestD = d;
            best = ci;
          }
        });
        if (assign[i] !== best) {
          assign[i] = best;
          changed = true;
        }
      });
      centroids = centroids.map((c, ci) => {
        const members = vectors.filter((_, i) => assign[i] === ci);
        return members.length ? c.map((_, d) => mean(members.map((m) => m[d]))) : c;
      });
      if (!changed) break;
    }

    const clusters = centroids
      .map((_, ci) => {
        const members = pool.filter((_, i) => assign[i] === ci);
        if (!members.length) return null;
        const profile = ALL_COMORBIDITIES.map((name) => ({
          name,
          rate: mean(members.map((m) => (m.comorbidities.includes(name) ? 1 : 0))),
        })).sort((a, b) => b.rate - a.rate);
        const hallmark = profile.filter((p) => p.rate >= 0.35).slice(0, 3);
        const byCat = groupBy(members, (m) => m.category);
        const [dominantCategory, dominantGroup] = [...byCat.entries()].sort((a, b) => b[1].length - a[1].length)[0];
        const cluster: Cluster = {
          id: ci,
          n: members.length,
          hallmark: hallmark.length ? hallmark : profile.slice(0, 1),
          avgAge: mean(members.map((m) => m.age)),
          avgComCount: mean(members.map((m) => m.comorbidities.length)),
          escRate: mean(members.map((m) => (escalated(m) ? 1 : 0))),
          readmitRate: mean(members.map((m) => (m.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0))),
          avgLos: mean(members.map((m) => m.losDays)),
          dominantCategory,
          dominantCategoryShare: dominantGroup.length / members.length,
          caseIds: members.map((m) => m.id)
        };
        return cluster;
      })
      .filter((c): c is Cluster => c !== null)
      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);

    return { k, n: pool.length, clusters, ready: true };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const eligible = cases.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.length > 0).length;
    if (eligible < 24) return { level: "weak", gaps: ["too few cases with comorbidities to cluster"] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's comorbidities (as a multi-hot vector); param k (default 4, clamped 2–6).",
    process: "Runs deterministic k-means (up to 30 iterations, seeded from evenly-spaced points along a comorbidity-count sort — never randomly) so the same cohort always yields the same clusters.",
    output: "k, n, ready, and clusters {id, n, hallmark:[{name, rate}], avgAge, avgComCount, escRate, readmitRate, avgLos, dominantCategory, dominantCategoryShare} — each cluster is a co-occurring condition bundle with its outcomes.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Cluster escRate/readmitRate are modeled from comorbidity burden — clusters re-display the injection, not discovered risk.",
      "Centroids seed along a comorbidity-count sort — cluster ordering is biased toward burden gradient, not neutral initialization.",
      "Multi-hot vectors treat each condition equally; hallmark at rate ≥0.35 can rest on a handful of members.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Cluster outcomes are formulas of the clustering variable.",
    ],
  },
};
Polypharmacy

Sorts patients by how many medications they take (0–2, 3–4, and so on up to 9+) and reports how often each group gets worse or comes back within 90 days.

 {
  id: "polypharmacy",
  name: "Polypharmacy",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Escalation and 90-day readmission rates by number of active medications at admission, bucketed (0–2, 3–4, 5–6, 7–8, 9+).",
  requiredFields: ["polypharmacyCount", "timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const rate = (group: Case[], f: (c: Case) => number) =>
      group.length ? group.reduce((s, c) => s + f(c), 0) / group.length : 0;
    const rows = BUCKETS.map(([lo, hi]) => {
      const group = cases.filter((c) => c.polypharmacyCount >= lo && c.polypharmacyCount <= hi);
      return {
        label: hi === 99 ? `${lo}+` : `${lo}–${hi}`,
        n: group.length,
        escRate: rate(group, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
        readmitRate: rate(group, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
        caseIds: group.map((c) => c.id)
      };
    }).filter((b) => b.n > 0);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's polypharmacyCount (distinct medications active at admission), plus timeToEscalationDays and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Bucket cases into medication-count bands (0–2, 3–4, 5–6, 7–8, 9+), then compute the escalation rate and 90-day readmission rate within each band.",
    output: "rows: one per non-empty band with {label, n, escRate, readmitRate} — read as whether heavier treatment tracks with worse outcomes.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "polypharmacyCount appears verbatim in escProb and readmitProb (loaders) — the gradient is the generator's arithmetic, not pharmacology.",
      "Confounding by indication is built in: heavier-med patients are older and more comorbid by generator construction.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The band variable is a coefficient of the modeled outcomes — every row is circular.",
    ],
  },
};
Time-to-escalation

Among patients who got worse, it measures how many days that took, and reports the typical and average time for each condition category and each level of illness burden.

 {
  id: "time-to-escalation",
  name: "Time-to-escalation",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Among escalated cases, days from presentation to escalation — by category and by comorbidity burden (1, 2, 3, 4, 5+ conditions).",
  requiredFields: ["timeToEscalationDays", "comorbidities"],
  run(cases) {
    const esc = cases.filter((c) => escalated(c) && c.timeToEscalationDays !== null);
    const byCat = groupBy(esc, (c) => c.category);
    const byCom = groupBy(esc, (c) => `${Math.min(c.comorbidities.length, 5)}${c.comorbidities.length >= 5 ? "+" : ""} comorb.`);
    return {
      n: esc.length,
      caseIds: esc.map((c) => c.id),
      byCat: rowsFor(byCat),
      byCom: rowsFor(byCom).sort((a, b) => Number(a.key.split(" ")[0]) - Number(b.key.split(" ")[0])),
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const escN = cases.filter((c) => escalated(c)).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? escN / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`escalated cases: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Escalated cases only: timeToEscalationDays, category, and comorbidities.",
    process: "Filters to escalated cases with a known time, groups by category and by comorbidity burden (1 through 5+), and computes the median and mean days to escalation per group; sorted fastest median first.",
    output: "n plus byCat and byCom rows {key, n, median, mean} — how quickly care escalates, by clinical context.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "timeToEscalationDays is a modeled draw — injected conditions escalate in 1–5 days at 90%, so byCat medians restate the generator.",
      "The by-comorbidity-burden axis stratifies on an input of the tte formula — circular comparison.",
      "Conditioned on escalated cases only — selection bias; never-escalated patients are invisible to every median.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Times and their stratifier are both generator formulas.",

Escalation-free survival (KM)

Draws the classic survival-style curve for 'staying well': each step down is a patient getting worse, and patients who leave without getting worse simply stop being counted from that day. Done overall and per category.

 {
  id: "km-curve",
  name: "Escalation-free survival (KM)",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Kaplan–Meier curve for staying escalation-free, overall and by category. Escalations are events; discharges without escalation are right-censored at LOS.",
  requiredFields: ["timeToEscalationDays", "losDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const usable = cases.filter((c) => !escalated(c) || c.timeToEscalationDays !== null);
    const overallSteps = kaplanMeier(usable.map(toRecord));
    const byCat = groupBy(usable, (c) => c.category);
const catSeries = [...byCat.entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => {
        const steps = kaplanMeier(g.map(toRecord));
        return {
          cat,
          n: g.length,
          events: g.filter((c) => escalated(c)).length,
          steps,
          median: kmMedian(steps),
          caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id)
        };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 8)
      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n)
      .slice(0, 4);
    return {
      n: usable.length,
      events: usable.filter((c) => escalated(c)).length,
      overallSteps,
      overallMedian: kmMedian(overallSteps),
      catSeries,
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's timeToEscalationDays and losDays.",
    process: "Builds a Kaplan–Meier curve for staying escalation-free: escalations are events at their day, discharges without escalation are right-censored at LOS; computed overall and per category (categories with n ≥ 8, top 4 by size).",
    output: "n, events, overallSteps, overallMedian, and catSeries {cat, n, events, steps, median} — a dropping curve means escalations accumulating over that day range.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "timeToEscalationDays is a modeled draw (randInt 1–5 for injected conditions, 1–14 otherwise) — the curve traces the generator's ranges.",
      "Non-escalated cases censor at losDays (randInt 1–21) — censoring is deterministic arithmetic, not loss to follow-up.",
      "Category series cap at top-4 by size with n≥8 — small categories vanish instead of showing their instability.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Events and censoring both come from generator formulas — survival estimates are simulation parameters.",
    ],
  },
};
Symptom proximity

For each condition category, finds the symptom that shows up much more often there than in the cohort overall — the symptom that 'belongs' to that category.

 {
  id: "symptom-proximity",
  name: "Symptom proximity",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "For each category, the symptom most enriched relative to the cohort-wide rate — the symptom that clusters around that category more than chance would suggest.",
  requiredFields: ["symptoms"],
  run(cases) {
    const allSymptoms = new Map<string, number>();
    cases.forEach((c) => c.symptoms.forEach((s) => allSymptoms.set(s, (allSymptoms.get(s) ?? 0) + 1)));
    const total = cases.length;
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    const rows: Array<{
      cat: string; sym: string; pctCat: number; pctAll: number; enrich: number;
      caseIds: string[];
    }> = [];
    byCat.forEach((group, cat) => {
      let best: (typeof rows)[number] | null = null;
      const symCount = new Map<string, number>();
      const symCases = new Map<string, Set<string>>(); // symptom -> case IDs
      group.forEach((c) => {
        c.symptoms.forEach((s) => {
          symCount.set(s, (symCount.get(s) ?? 0) + 1);
          if (!symCases.has(s)) symCases.set(s, new Set());
          symCases.get(s)!.add(c.id);
        });
      });
      symCount.forEach((n, sym) => {
        if (n < MIN_N) return;
        const pctCat = n / group.length;
        const pctAll = (allSymptoms.get(sym) ?? 0) / total;
        const enrich = pctAll > 0 ? pctCat / pctAll : 0;
        if (!best || enrich > best.enrich) {
          best = { 
            cat, 
            sym, 
            pctCat, 
            pctAll, 
            enrich,
            caseIds: Array.from(symCases.get(sym) ?? [])
          };
        }
      });
      if (best) rows.push(best);
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.enrich - a.enrich) };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withSym = cases.filter((c) => c.symptoms.length > 0).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withSym / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`cases with symptoms: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's symptoms and category.",
    process: "For each category, finds the symptom with at least 4 in-category cases whose in-category rate ÷ cohort-wide rate (enrichment) is highest.",
    output: "rows: {cat, sym, pctCat, pctAll, enrich} — enrichment > 1 means the symptom clusters around that category more than baseline.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "ok",
    points: [
      "Enrichment with MIN_N=4: a four-case symptom can top a category — enrichment >1 is often sampling noise.",
      "Best-of-scan per category with no multiplicity correction; still descriptive-only (no outcome claim).",
    ],
  },
};
Readmission drivers

Like Backward lift but pointed at coming back to the hospital: for each condition category, finds the co-existing condition that most increases the chance of a 90-day readmission.

 {
  id: "readmission-drivers",
  name: "Readmission drivers",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Per category, the comorbidity most lifting the 90-day readmission rate — the backward-lift arithmetic pointed at readmission instead of escalation.",
  requiredFields: ["category", "comorbidities", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    const rows: Array<{
      cat: string; com: string; n: number; rWith: number; rWithout: number; lift: number; caseIds: string[];
    }> = [];
    byCat.forEach((group, cat) => {
      let best: (typeof rows)[number] | null = null;
      for (const com of ALL_COMORBIDITIES) {
        if (com === cat) continue;
        const withCom = group.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.includes(com));
        const withoutCom = group.filter((c) => !c.comorbidities.includes(com));
        if (withCom.length < 4 || withoutCom.length < 2) continue;
        const rWith = withCom.filter((c) => c.readmitted90d).length / withCom.length;
        const rWithout = withoutCom.filter((c) => c.readmitted90d).length / withoutCom.length;
        const lift = rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : 0;
        if (!best || lift > best.lift) best = { cat, com, n: withCom.length, rWith, rWithout, lift, caseIds: withCom.map((c) => c.id) };
      }
      if (best) rows.push(best);
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.lift - a.lift).slice(0, 10) };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's category, comorbidities, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Within each category, scans every comorbidity with at least 4 patients having it and 2 without, computes the readmission rate with vs without, and keeps the comorbidity with the highest lift (with ÷ without).",
    output: "rows: top 10 {cat, com, n, rWith, rWithout, lift} — backward-lift arithmetic pointed at readmission instead of escalation.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "readmitProb = 0.12 + comorbidities*0.04 + polypharmacy*0.015 in the loaders — the winning comorbidity is the formula's own coefficient.",
      "Max-of-scan over ALL_COMORBIDITIES with min n 4/2 — winner's curse on small cells.",
      "lift collapses to 0 when rWithout is 0, silently hiding uncomputable rows instead of flagging them.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Comorbidity count is a direct input of readmitProb — lifts restate the generator.",
    ],
  },
};
Equity check

Compares outcomes across groups of people — sex, age, insurance, region, subgroup — to check whether any group fares noticeably worse than the others.

 {
  id: "equity",
  name: "Equity check",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Escalation and readmission rates across demographic and access dimensions — sex, age band, insurance, region, and subgroup — to check whether any outcome pattern skews toward a population segment.",
  requiredFields: ["sex", "age", "insurance", "region", "subgroup"],
  run(cases) {
    const bands: Array<[number, number]> = [[45, 54], [55, 64], [65, 74], [75, 99]];
    const ageRows = bands
      .map(([lo, hi]) => {
        const g = cases.filter((c) => c.age >= lo && c.age <= hi);
        return { k: hi === 99 ? `${lo}+` : `${lo}–${hi}`, n: g.length, escRate: mean(g.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))), caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id) };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n > 0);
    return {
      sexRows: groupRows(cases, (c) => c.sex, false, true),
      ageRows,
      insuranceRows: groupRows(cases, (c) => c.insurance, true),
      regionRows: groupRows(cases, (c) => c.region),
      subgroupRows: groupRows(cases, (c) => c.subgroup),
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's sex, age, insurance, region, subgroup, escalation outcome, readmitted90d, and losDays.",
    process: "Groups the cohort separately by sex, age band (45–54 through 75+), insurance, region, and subgroup, computing escalation rate in every group — plus mean LOS for sex and readmission rate for insurance.",
    output: "five arrays (sexRows, ageRows, insuranceRows, regionRows, subgroupRows) of {k, n, escRate, readmit?, los?} — scan for any segment whose rates skew.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Age band feeds escProb directly, and outcome rates are modeled — any age skew is injected, not observed disparity.",
      "No race/ethnicity field exists; insurance, region, and subgroup rows are socioeconomic proxies, not protected-class equity analysis.",
      "Rows sort by n, not reliability — single-digit cells sit beside cohort-scale rates with no small-n flag.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Stratifiers either feed the outcome formulas (age) or proxy disadvantage (insurance/region/subgroup) — treat gaps as generator artifacts.",
    ],
  },
};
High-utilizers

Takes the 10% of patients with the most hospital visits in 90 days and compares them to everyone else: are they sicker, do they get worse more often, do they stay longer?

 {
  id: "high-utilizers",
  name: "High-utilizers",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "The top decile of the cohort by 90-day encounter count, profiled against everyone else on escalation, readmission, comorbidity burden, medications, and length of stay.",
  requiredFields: ["encounters90d", "timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const sorted = [...cases].sort((a, b) => b.encounters90d - a.encounters90d);
    const cut = Math.max(1, Math.round(cases.length * 0.1));
    const hi = sorted.slice(0, cut);
    const rest = sorted.slice(cut);
    const byCatHi = groupBy(hi, (c) => c.category);
    const catRows = [...byCatHi.entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => ({ cat, n: g.length, caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id) }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
    return { hi: summarize(hi), rest: summarize(rest), catRows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's encounters90d plus escalation, readmitted90d, comorbidities, polypharmacyCount, and losDays.",
    process: "Sorts by 90-day encounter count, takes the top decile (minimum 1 patient), and profiles that group against everyone else on mean encounters, escalation, readmission, comorbidities, medications, and LOS; also counts the top decile per category.",
    output: "hi and rest summaries {n, enc90, escRate, readmit, com, meds, los}, plus catRows {cat, n} — the utilization-outcome contrast at the top decile.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "encounters90d is a modeled draw (randInt(1,12) in synthetic.ts; capped Encounter count in fhir.ts) — the top decile is a generator artifact.",
      "Grouping variable and outcomes (escRate, readmit) are both formula outputs — the contrast is circular.",
      "Fixed top-decile cut has no clinical threshold; membership flips with cohort size.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Utilization is modeled, not observed — do not use the hi/rest contrast for targeting.",
    ],
  },
};
SDOH

For each social barrier (transportation, medication cost, housing, food), compares patients who have it with patients who have none at all, on getting worse and coming back.

 {
  id: "sdoh",
  name: "SDOH",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Escalation and readmission rates for patients carrying each flagged social-determinant barrier (transportation, medication cost, housing instability, food insecurity) versus unflagged patients.",
  requiredFields: ["sdohBarriers", "timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const unflagged = cases.filter((c) => c.sdohBarriers.length === 0);
    const rate = (g: Case[], f: (c: Case) => number) => (g.length ? g.reduce((s, c) => s + f(c), 0) / g.length : 0);
    const barriers = [...new Set(cases.flatMap((c) => c.sdohBarriers))].sort();
    const rows = barriers.map((b) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => c.sdohBarriers.includes(b));
      return {
        barrier: b,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
        readmit: rate(g, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
        unflaggedEsc: rate(unflagged, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
        unflaggedReadmit: rate(unflagged, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
        caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id)
      };
    });
    return { rows, unflaggedN: unflagged.length };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const flagged = cases.filter((c) => c.sdohBarriers.length > 0).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? flagged / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.1) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`flagged patients: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    if (pct < 0.25) return { level: "limited", gaps: [`flagged patients: only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's sdohBarriers, escalation outcome, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "For each distinct SDOH barrier, computes escalation and readmission rates among patients carrying it and among patients with no barriers at all, for direct contrast.",
    output: "rows: one per barrier with {barrier, n, escRate, readmit, unflaggedEsc, unflaggedReadmit}, plus unflaggedN — each barrier type against the unflagged baseline.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "sdohBarriers drive the generator's own flags (missedFollowup, refillGap, vaccinated) — barrier rows partly measure the generator's wiring.",
      "Barriers are a list: multi-barrier patients appear in several rows and the unflagged baseline mixes everyone else.",
      "No adjustment: barrier carriers differ in burden the generator also models into outcomes — confounding by indication.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Barrier-stratified escalation/readmission restates formulas the barriers feed or correlate with.",
    ],
  },
};

Diagnostic ambiguity

Finds pairs of symptoms that appear across many different condition categories — the more categories share a symptom pair, the less that pair helps pin down a diagnosis.

 {
  id: "diagnostic-ambiguity",
  name: "Diagnostic ambiguity",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "Symptom pairs that occur across multiple diagnostic categories — the more categories share a symptom pair, the more ambiguous that presentation is as a diagnostic signal.",
  requiredFields: ["symptoms", "category"],
run(cases) {
    const setMap = new Map<string, Map<string, number>>();
    const caseIdsMap = new Map<string, string[]>();
    cases.forEach((c) => {
      if (c.symptoms.length < 2) return;
      const sorted = [...c.symptoms].sort();
      for (let i = 0; i < sorted.length; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < sorted.length; j++) {
          const key = `${sorted[i]} + ${sorted[j]}`;
          if (!setMap.has(key)) setMap.set(key, new Map());
          const catMap = setMap.get(key)!;
          catMap.set(c.category, (catMap.get(c.category) ?? 0) + 1);
          if (!caseIdsMap.has(key)) caseIdsMap.set(key, []);
          caseIdsMap.get(key)!.push(c.id);
        }
      }
    });
    const rows: Array<{
      key: string; n: number; nConds: number; topCat: string; topN: number;
      dist: Array<{ cat: string; n: number }>; caseIds: string[];
    }> = [];
    setMap.forEach((catMap, key) => {
      const total = [...catMap.values()].reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
      if (total < MIN_TOTAL || catMap.size < 2) return;
      const dist = [...catMap.entries()].sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1]).map(([cat, n]) => ({ cat, n }));
      rows.push({ key, n: total, nConds: catMap.size, topCat: dist[0].cat, topN: dist[0].n, dist, caseIds: caseIdsMap.get(key) ?? [] });
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.nConds - a.nConds || b.n - a.n).slice(0, 8) };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const eligible = cases.filter((c) => c.symptoms.length >= 2).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? eligible / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.15) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`multi-symptom cases: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    if (pct < 0.4) return { level: "limited", gaps: [`multi-symptom cases: only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's symptoms and category.",
    process: "Builds every symptom pair within multi-symptom cases, counts how many diagnostic categories each pair appears in, keeps pairs seen ≥ 6 times across ≥ 2 categories, and ranks by category count.",
    output: "rows: top 8 with {key, n, nConds, topCat, topN, dist} — the more categories share a pair, the more ambiguous that presentation is.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "ok",
    points: [
      "Pure pair counting, no outcome claim — but pairs share patients across categories, so nConds tracks symptom prevalence, not true ambiguity.",
      "MIN_TOTAL=6 and the top-8 cut are arbitrary; a common pair can dominate without being clinically confusable.",
    ],
  },
};

 {

    output: "rows: one per category with {cat, n, adherentRate, escRateAdherent, escRateNonAdherent} — the adherence-outcome gap per category.",

Build your own query

Lets you build your own patient filter from simple rules — include these, exclude those — and then compares the matched patients to everyone else. This is the same engine the LLM writes test queries for.

 {
  id: "query-engine",
  name: "Build your own query",
  category: "Records",
  description:
    "The general engine behind every fixed analytic: AND-stack any coded filters, run them as deterministic code, and compare the matched group to the rest of the cohort on outcomes.",
  requiredFields: ["timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d", "losDays"],
  run(cases, params) {
    let parsed: unknown = [];
    try {
      parsed = params?.query ? JSON.parse(params.query) : [];
    } catch {
      return { error: "malformed query parameter" };
    }
    // Accept both new QbQuery and legacy QbRow[] formats.
    const isQbQuery =
      parsed && typeof parsed === "object" && "include" in parsed && "exclude" in parsed;
    let q: QbQuery;
    if (isQbQuery) {
      q = parsed as QbQuery;
    } else {
      const flatRows = (Array.isArray(parsed) ? parsed : []) as QbRow[];
      const cleaned = flatRows.filter((r) => QB_FIELDS.some((f) => f.key === r.field));
      q = fromLegacyRows(cleaned);
    }
    const matched = cases.filter((c) => applyQuery(c, q));
    const rest = cases.filter((c) => !matched.includes(c));
    const escRate = rate(matched, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0));
    const escRateRest = rate(rest, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0));
    const readmit = rate(matched, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0));
    const readmitRest = rate(rest, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0));
    return {
      appliedFilters: isQbQuery ? null : (Array.isArray(parsed) ? (parsed as QbRow[]) : []),
      n: matched.length,
      nRest: rest.length,
      escRate,
      escRateRest,
      escLift: escRateRest > 0 ? escRate / escRateRest : escRate > 0 ? 99 : 0,
      readmit,
      readmitRest,
      readmitLift: readmitRest > 0 ? readmit / readmitRest : readmit > 0 ? 99 : 0,
      meanLos: rate(matched, (c) => c.losDays),
      meanLosRest: rate(rest, (c) => c.losDays),
      meanEnc: rate(matched, (c) => c.encounters90d),
      meanEncRest: rate(rest, (c) => c.encounters90d),
      caseIds: matched.map((c) => c.id)
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "A query param (nested QbQuery JSON or legacy filter rows) over the coded field catalog; per-case escalation, readmitted90d, losDays, encounters90d.",
    process: "Evaluates the include-AND-NOT-exclude filter tree as deterministic code (case-insensitive substring 'includes' on list fields, numeric gte/lte, temporal date ops), then compares the matched group to the rest of the cohort.",
    output: "appliedFilters, n/nRest, escRate/escRateRest and escLift, readmit/readmitRest and readmitLift, meanLos/meanLosRest, meanEnc/meanEncRest — lift > 1 means the matched group fares worse.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Subsumption matching pulls in descendants silently — 'includes' on a SNOMED ancestor can match far more cases than the display string suggests.",
      "Any filter touching age, comorbidities, polypharmacy, SDOH or screening compares groups the generator already made different — lifts restate escProb.",
      "escLift/readmitLift carry no minimum matched n — a two-case filter yields a finite-looking lift.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Outcome lifts over modeled outcomes are circular; subsumption widens the matched set invisibly.",
    ],
  },
};
Pharmacovigilance

Looks only at patients on 5+ medications, reports how often each category gets worse, and flags medications that show up unusually often among the patients who got worse.

 {
  id: "pharmacovigilance",
  name: "Pharmacovigilance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Restricted to patients on 5+ active medications: escalation rates by category, sorted worst-first, with the medication most shared among escalated cases.",
  requiredFields: ["polypharmacyCount", "timeToEscalationDays", "medications"],
  run(cases) {
    const poly = cases.filter((c) => c.polypharmacyCount >= 5);
    const rows = [...groupBy(poly, (c) => c.category).entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => ({
        cat,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: g.filter(escalated).length / g.length,
        caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id)
      }))
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate)
      .slice(0, 8);
    // top medication among escalated polypharmacy patients vs non-escalated
    const esc = poly.filter(escalated);
    const nonEsc = poly.filter((c) => !escalated(c));
    const medRate = (g: Case[]) => {
      const m = new Map<string, number>();
      g.forEach((c) => c.medications.forEach((x) => m.set(x, (m.get(x) ?? 0) + 1)));
      return m;
    };
    const escMeds = medRate(esc);
    const nonEscMeds = medRate(nonEsc);
    const signals = [...escMeds.entries()]
      .map(([med, n]) => {
        const escCasesWithMed = esc.filter((c) => c.medications.includes(med));
        return {
        med,
        escShare: n / Math.max(esc.length, 1),
        ratio: n / Math.max(esc.length, 1) / Math.max((nonEscMeds.get(med) ?? 0) / Math.max(nonEsc.length, 1), 0.0001),
        caseIds: escCasesWithMed.map((c) => c.id)
        };
      })
      .sort((a, b) => b.ratio - a.ratio)
      .slice(0, 5);
    return { nPoly: poly.length, rows, signals };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const poly = cases.filter((c) => c.polypharmacyCount >= 5).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? poly / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.1) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`polypharmacy patients: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's polypharmacyCount, medications, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Restricts to patients on 5+ active medications, computes escalation rate per category (n ≥ 5, top 8), then for each medication compares its share among escalated vs non-escalated polypharmacy patients as a ratio.",
    output: "nPoly, rows {cat, n, escRate}, and signals: top 5 {med, escShare, ratio} — ratio > 1 means the drug is over-represented among escalated cases (associational only).",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "The 5+ medication screen stratifies on an escProb term (polypharmacy adds min(count,8)*0.02) — escalation rates here are pre-injected.",
      "Medication 'signals' compare shares among modeled escalated vs not — ecological ratios with no patient-level matching.",
      "ratio divides by the non-escalated share with no floor — a drug seen once among non-escalated patients explodes to the top.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Denominator (polypharmacy) is a cause of the modeled outcome — circular by construction.",
    ],
  },
};
Surveillance

Counts admissions per calendar month and how often patients get worse each month, then flags the busiest month.

 {
  id: "surveillance",
  name: "Surveillance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Admissions and escalation rates by calendar month, with the peak month flagged — the population-health seasonal view.",
  requiredFields: ["admitMonth", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const byMonth = groupBy(cases, (c) => String(c.admitMonth));
    const rows = [];
    for (let m = 1; m <= 12; m++) {
      const g = byMonth.get(String(m)) ?? [];
      rows.push({ month: MONTHS[m - 1], n: g.length, escRate: mean(g.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))), caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id) });
    }
    const peak = [...rows].sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n)[0];
    return { rows, peak };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's admitMonth and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Groups admissions by calendar month (Jan–Dec), counts admissions and computes the escalation rate per month, and flags the month with the most admissions.",
    output: "rows: one per month {month, n, escRate}, plus peak — the seasonal-signal view; months with no admissions show n = 0.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "admitMonth is a uniform randInt(1,12) draw — the flagged 'peak' month is the largest sample of a uniform variable.",
      "escRate per month rides a modeled outcome on ~1/12 of one snapshot — pure small-n noise.",
    ],
  },
};
A vs. B drift

Compares the two data arms (A and B) on the headline numbers and shows where they differ most — a simple check for drift between datasets.

 {
  id: "drift",
  name: "A vs. B drift",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Compares dataset arm A (bulk) against arm B (comparison sample) on headline metrics — cohort size, escalation, age, comorbidity burden, LOS — and lists the largest per-category escalation gaps.",
  requiredFields: ["dataset", "timeToEscalationDays"],
run(cases) {
    const A = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "A");
    const B = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B");
    const metrics: DriftMetrics[] = [
      { label: "n", a: A.length, b: B.length },
      { label: "Escalation rate", a: mean(A.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))), b: mean(B.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))) },
      { label: "Avg age", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.age)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.age)) },
      { label: "Avg comorbidities", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.comorbidities.length)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.comorbidities.length)) },
      { label: "Avg LOS (days)", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.losDays)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.losDays)) },
    ];
    const catRows: DriftCatRow[] = [...groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category).entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => {
        const a = g.filter((c) => c.dataset === "A");
        const b = g.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B");
        const ea = mean(a.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)));
        const eb = mean(b.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)));
        return { cat, na: a.length, nb: b.length, ea, eb, diff: Math.abs(ea - eb), caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id) };
      })
      .sort((x, y) => y.diff - x.diff);
    return { metrics, catRows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const b = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B").length;
    const pct = cases.length ? b / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.05) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`arm B covers only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort`] };
    if (pct < 0.15) return { level: "limited", gaps: [`arm B covers only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's dataset field (arm A = bulk, arm B = comparison sample) plus age, comorbidities, losDays, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Splits the cohort into arms A and B, compares headline metrics (n, escalation rate, mean age, mean comorbidities, mean LOS) side by side, then ranks per-category escalation gaps by absolute difference.",
    output: "metrics: {label, a, b} and catRows: {cat, na, nb, ea, eb, diff} — read as whether arm A and arm B tell the same story.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Arms A and B are two arms of the same generator — this can only detect generator drift between arms, never real-world drift.",
      "Per-category gaps rank on absolute difference with no n or CI — small-category noise tops the list.",
      "Escalation rates are formula outputs; a category 'gap' can be a planted INJECTED_LIFT difference.",
    ],
  },
};
Region × insurance

Builds a table of every region-and-insurance combination and reports outcomes in each cell, showing where access disadvantages stack up.

 {
  id: "region-insurance",
  name: "Region × insurance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Escalation and readmission rates for every region × insurance combination — where access disadvantage compounds.",
  requiredFields: ["region", "insurance", "timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const rows: RegionInsuranceRow[] = cases
      .map((c) => `${c.region}|${c.insurance}`)
      .filter((k, i, arr) => arr.indexOf(k) === i)
      .map((k) => {
        const [region, insurance] = k.split("|");
        const g = cases.filter((c) => c.region === region && c.insurance === insurance);
        return {
          label: `${region} × ${insurance}`,
          n: g.length,
          escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
          readmitRate: rate(g, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
          caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id),
        };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's region, insurance, timeToEscalationDays, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Cross-tabs every region × insurance combination with at least 5 cases, computing escalation and 90-day readmission rates in each cell; sorted by escalation rate descending.",
    output: "rows: one per combination with {label, n, escRate, readmitRate} — where access disadvantage compounds with outcomes.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Region × insurance is a socioeconomic proxy — no race/ethnicity field exists, so this is not protected-class equity analysis.",
      "escRate/readmitRate are modeled; any cell gap is generator-injected, and n≥5 cells are too small to read.",
      "Cells with n<5 are silently dropped, biasing the visible table toward common strata.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Stratifiers proxy disadvantage and outcomes are modeled — gaps are artifacts.",
    ],
  },
};

Cross-tabulates age band, sex and subgroup all at once, and shows the twelve combinations with the worst escalation rates (only combinations with enough patients to read).

 {

  requiredFields: ["age", "sex", "subgroup", "timeToEscalationDays"],

          caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id),

      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate)
      .slice(0, 12);
    return { rows };
  },

    output: "rows: one per stratum with {label, n, escRate, readmitRate} — the three-way stratification view.",
  },
  audit: {

Seasonal pattern

Groups patients by season of admission and reports how the mix of conditions and the escalation rate change with the calendar.

 {
  id: "seasonal-pattern",
  name: "Seasonal pattern",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Category mix and escalation rate by season (winter = Dec–Feb), to see whether the cohort's composition shifts with the calendar.",
  requiredFields: ["admitMonth", "category", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const seasonOf = (m: number) =>
      m === 12 || m <= 2 ? "Winter" : m <= 5 ? "Spring" : m <= 8 ? "Summer" : "Fall";
    const seasons = ["Winter", "Spring", "Summer", "Fall"];
    const rows: SeasonalPatternRow[] = seasons.map((s) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => seasonOf(c.admitMonth) === s);
      const byCat = groupBy(g, (c) => c.category);
      const mix = [...byCat.entries()]
        .map(([cat, grp]) => ({ cat, share: g.length ? grp.length / g.length : 0 }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.share - a.share);
      return {
        label: s,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
        topCategory: mix[0]?.cat ?? "—",
        topShare: mix[0]?.share ?? 0,
        caseIds: g.map((c) => c.id),
      };
    });
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's admitMonth, category, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Groups cases by season derived from admitMonth (Winter = Dec–Feb, then Spring/Summer/Fall), and per season counts cases, computes escalation rate, and reports the most common category and its share.",
    output: "rows: one per season with {label, n, escRate, topCategory, topShare} — whether cohort composition shifts with the calendar.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "admitMonth is a uniform randInt(1,12) — any season gradient is sampling noise, not epidemiology.",
      "One admission snapshot backs every season; no longitudinal follow-up exists.",
      "topCategory share in small seasons flips with a handful of cases.",
    ],
  },
};

Symptom → investigation

For each common symptom, checks how often any follow-up test was ordered at all — a scan for missed work-ups, worst first.

 {
  id: "symptom-investigation",
  name: "Symptom → investigation",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "For each common symptom, how often the work-ups that should follow it actually get ordered — the missed-work-up scan.",
  requiredFields: ["symptoms", "investigations"],
  run(cases) {
    const counts = new Map<string, { n: number; anyInvest: number; caseIds: string[] }>();
    for (const c of cases) {
      for (const s of c.symptoms) {
        let rec = counts.get(s);
        if (!rec) {
          rec = { n: 0, anyInvest: 0, caseIds: [] };
          counts.set(s, rec);
        }
        rec.n++;
        rec.caseIds.push(c.id);
        if (c.investigations.length > 0) rec.anyInvest++;
      }
    }
    const rows = [...counts.entries()]
      .filter(([, r]) => r.n >= 15)
      .map(([sym, r]) => ({
        label: sym,
        n: r.n,
        investigatedRate: r.anyInvest / r.n,
        caseIds: r.caseIds,
      }))
      .sort((a, b) => a.investigatedRate - b.investigatedRate);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's symptoms and investigations.",
