Build your own query

Lets you build your own patient filter from simple rules — include these, exclude those — and then compares the matched patients to everyone else. This is the same engine the LLM writes test queries for.

 {
  id: "query-engine",
  name: "Build your own query",
  category: "Records",
  description:
    "The general engine behind every fixed analytic: AND-stack any coded filters, run them as deterministic code, and compare the matched group to the rest of the cohort on outcomes.",
  requiredFields: ["timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d", "losDays"],
  run(cases, params) {
    const mode = (params?.mode === "v2" ? "v2" : "v1") as "v1" | "v2";
    const ecl = params?.ecl ?? "";
    let q: QbQuery;
    let appliedFilters: unknown = null;
    let parseError: string | undefined;
    if (ecl) {
      // ECL string → QbQuery (V2 path).
      try {
        q = parseEcl(ecl);
        appliedFilters = { ecl };
      } catch (err) {
        return { error: `ECL parse error: ${(err as Error).message}`, mode };
      }
    } else {
      // Legacy QbQuery / QbRow[] JSON.
      let parsed: unknown = [];
      try {
        parsed = params?.query ? JSON.parse(params.query) : [];
      } catch {
        return { error: "malformed query parameter", mode };
      }
      const isQbQuery =
        parsed && typeof parsed === "object" && "include" in parsed && "exclude" in parsed;
      if (isQbQuery) {
        q = parsed as QbQuery;
      } else {
        const flatRows = (Array.isArray(parsed) ? parsed : []) as QbRow[];
        const cleaned = flatRows.filter((r) => QB_FIELDS.some((f) => f.key === r.field));
        q = fromLegacyRows(cleaned);
        appliedFilters = isQbQuery ? null : (Array.isArray(parsed) ? (parsed as QbRow[]) : []);
      }
    }
    const matched = cases.filter((c) => applyQuery(c, q, mode));
    const rest = cases.filter((c) => !matched.includes(c));
    const escRate = rate(matched, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0));
    const escRateRest = rate(rest, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0));
    const readmit = rate(matched, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0));
    const readmitRest = rate(rest, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0));
    return {
      mode,
      appliedFilters,
      n: matched.length,
      nRest: rest.length,
      escRate,
      escRateRest,
      escLift: escRateRest > 0 ? escRate / escRateRest : escRate > 0 ? 99 : 0,
      readmit,
      readmitRest,
      readmitLift: readmitRest > 0 ? readmit / readmitRest : readmit > 0 ? 99 : 0,
      meanLos: rate(matched, (c) => c.losDays),
      meanLosRest: rate(rest, (c) => c.losDays),
      meanEnc: rate(matched, (c) => c.encounters90d),
      meanEncRest: rate(rest, (c) => c.encounters90d),
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "A query param (nested QbQuery JSON or legacy filter rows) over the coded field catalog; per-case escalation, readmitted90d, losDays, encounters90d.",
    process: "Evaluates the include-AND-NOT-exclude filter tree as deterministic code (case-insensitive substring 'includes' on list fields, numeric gte/lte, temporal date ops), then compares the matched group to the rest of the cohort.",
    output: "appliedFilters, n/nRest, escRate/escRateRest and escLift, readmit/readmitRest and readmitLift, meanLos/meanLosRest, meanEnc/meanEncRest — lift > 1 means the matched group fares worse.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Subsumption matching pulls in descendants silently — 'includes' on a SNOMED ancestor can match far more cases than the display string suggests.",
      "Any filter touching age, comorbidities, polypharmacy, SDOH or screening compares groups the generator already made different — lifts restate escProb.",
      "escLift/readmitLift carry no minimum matched n — a two-case filter yields a finite-looking lift.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Outcome lifts over modeled outcomes are circular; subsumption widens the matched set invisibly.",
    ],
  },
};
Pharmacovigilance

Looks only at patients on 5+ medications, reports how often each category gets worse, and flags medications that show up unusually often among the patients who got worse.

 {
  id: "pharmacovigilance",
  name: "Pharmacovigilance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Restricted to patients on 5+ active medications: escalation rates by category, sorted worst-first, with the medication most shared among escalated cases.",
  requiredFields: ["polypharmacyCount", "timeToEscalationDays", "medications"],
  run(cases) {
    const poly = cases.filter((c) => c.polypharmacyCount >= 5);
    const rows = [...groupBy(poly, (c) => c.category).entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => ({
        cat,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: g.filter(escalated).length / g.length,
      }))
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate)
      .slice(0, 8);
    // top medication among escalated polypharmacy patients vs non-escalated
    const esc = poly.filter(escalated);
    const nonEsc = poly.filter((c) => !escalated(c));
    const medRate = (g: Case[]) => {
      const m = new Map<string, number>();
      g.forEach((c) => c.medications.forEach((x) => m.set(x, (m.get(x) ?? 0) + 1)));
      return m;
    };
    const escMeds = medRate(esc);
    const nonEscMeds = medRate(nonEsc);
    const signals = [...escMeds.entries()]
      .map(([med, n]) => ({
        med,
        escShare: n / Math.max(esc.length, 1),
        ratio: n / Math.max(esc.length, 1) / Math.max((nonEscMeds.get(med) ?? 0) / Math.max(nonEsc.length, 1), 0.0001),
      }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.ratio - a.ratio)
      .slice(0, 5);
    return { nPoly: poly.length, rows, signals };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const poly = cases.filter((c) => c.polypharmacyCount >= 5).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? poly / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.1) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`polypharmacy patients: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's polypharmacyCount, medications, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Restricts to patients on 5+ active medications, computes escalation rate per category (n ≥ 5, top 8), then for each medication compares its share among escalated vs non-escalated polypharmacy patients as a ratio.",
    output: "nPoly, rows {cat, n, escRate}, and signals: top 5 {med, escShare, ratio} — ratio > 1 means the drug is over-represented among escalated cases (associational only).",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "The 5+ medication screen stratifies on an escProb term (polypharmacy adds min(count,8)*0.02) — escalation rates here are pre-injected.",
      "Medication 'signals' compare shares among modeled escalated vs not — ecological ratios with no patient-level matching.",
      "ratio divides by the non-escalated share with no floor — a drug seen once among non-escalated patients explodes to the top.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Denominator (polypharmacy) is a cause of the modeled outcome — circular by construction.",
    ],
  },
};
Surveillance

Counts admissions per calendar month and how often patients get worse each month, then flags the busiest month.

 {
  id: "surveillance",
  name: "Surveillance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Admissions and escalation rates by calendar month, with the peak month flagged — the population-health seasonal view.",
  requiredFields: ["admitMonth", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const byMonth = groupBy(cases, (c) => String(c.admitMonth));
    const rows = [];
    for (let m = 1; m <= 12; m++) {
      const g = byMonth.get(String(m)) ?? [];
      rows.push({ month: MONTHS[m - 1], n: g.length, escRate: mean(g.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))) });
    }
    const peak = [...rows].sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n)[0];
    return { rows, peak };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's admitMonth and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Groups admissions by calendar month (Jan–Dec), counts admissions and computes the escalation rate per month, and flags the month with the most admissions.",
    output: "rows: one per month {month, n, escRate}, plus peak — the seasonal-signal view; months with no admissions show n = 0.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "admitMonth is a uniform randInt(1,12) draw — the flagged 'peak' month is the largest sample of a uniform variable.",
      "escRate per month rides a modeled outcome on ~1/12 of one snapshot — pure small-n noise.",
    ],
  },
};
A vs. B drift

Compares the two data arms (A and B) on the headline numbers and shows where they differ most — a simple check for drift between datasets.

 {
  id: "drift",
  name: "A vs. B drift",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Compares dataset arm A (bulk) against arm B (comparison sample) on headline metrics — cohort size, escalation, age, comorbidity burden, LOS — and lists the largest per-category escalation gaps.",
  requiredFields: ["dataset", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const A = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "A");
    const B = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B");
    const metrics = [
      { label: "n", a: A.length, b: B.length },
      { label: "Escalation rate", a: mean(A.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))), b: mean(B.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0))) },
      { label: "Avg age", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.age)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.age)) },
      { label: "Avg comorbidities", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.comorbidities.length)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.comorbidities.length)) },
      { label: "Avg LOS (days)", a: mean(A.map((c) => c.losDays)), b: mean(B.map((c) => c.losDays)) },
    ];
    const catRows = [...groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category).entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => {
        const a = g.filter((c) => c.dataset === "A");
        const b = g.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B");
        const ea = mean(a.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)));
        const eb = mean(b.map((c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)));
        return { cat, na: a.length, nb: b.length, ea, eb, diff: Math.abs(ea - eb) };
      })
      .sort((x, y) => y.diff - x.diff);
    return { metrics, catRows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const b = cases.filter((c) => c.dataset === "B").length;
    const pct = cases.length ? b / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.05) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`arm B covers only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort`] };
    if (pct < 0.15) return { level: "limited", gaps: [`arm B covers only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's dataset field (arm A = bulk, arm B = comparison sample) plus age, comorbidities, losDays, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Splits the cohort into arms A and B, compares headline metrics (n, escalation rate, mean age, mean comorbidities, mean LOS) side by side, then ranks per-category escalation gaps by absolute difference.",
    output: "metrics: {label, a, b} and catRows: {cat, na, nb, ea, eb, diff} — read as whether arm A and arm B tell the same story.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Arms A and B are two arms of the same generator — this can only detect generator drift between arms, never real-world drift.",
      "Per-category gaps rank on absolute difference with no n or CI — small-category noise tops the list.",
      "Escalation rates are formula outputs; a category 'gap' can be a planted INJECTED_LIFT difference.",
    ],
  },
};
Region × insurance

Builds a table of every region-and-insurance combination and reports outcomes in each cell, showing where access disadvantages stack up.

 {
  id: "region-insurance",
  name: "Region × insurance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Escalation and readmission rates for every region × insurance combination — where access disadvantage compounds.",
  requiredFields: ["region", "insurance", "timeToEscalationDays", "readmitted90d"],
  run(cases) {
    const rows = cases
      .map((c) => `${c.region}|${c.insurance}`)
      .filter((k, i, arr) => arr.indexOf(k) === i)
      .map((k) => {
        const [region, insurance] = k.split("|");
        const g = cases.filter((c) => c.region === region && c.insurance === insurance);
        return {
          label: `${region} × ${insurance}`,
          n: g.length,
          escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
          readmitRate: rate(g, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
        };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's region, insurance, timeToEscalationDays, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Cross-tabs every region × insurance combination with at least 5 cases, computing escalation and 90-day readmission rates in each cell; sorted by escalation rate descending.",
    output: "rows: one per combination with {label, n, escRate, readmitRate} — where access disadvantage compounds with outcomes.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Region × insurance is a socioeconomic proxy — no race/ethnicity field exists, so this is not protected-class equity analysis.",
      "escRate/readmitRate are modeled; any cell gap is generator-injected, and n≥5 cells are too small to read.",
      "Cells with n<5 are silently dropped, biasing the visible table toward common strata.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Stratifiers proxy disadvantage and outcomes are modeled — gaps are artifacts.",
    ],
  },
};
Age × sex × subgroup

Cross-tabulates age band, sex and subgroup all at once, and shows the twelve combinations with the worst escalation rates (only combinations with enough patients to read).

 {
  id: "triple-strat",
  name: "Age × sex × subgroup",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "The PoC's three-way stratification: escalation rate by age band, sex, and subgroup, for strata with enough patients to read.",
  requiredFields: ["age", "sex", "subgroup", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const band = (c: Case) => (c.age >= 75 ? "75+" : c.age >= 65 ? "65–74" : "45–64");
    const rows = cases
      .map((c) => `${band(c)}|${c.sex}|${c.subgroup}`)
      .filter((k, i, arr) => arr.indexOf(k) === i)
      .map((k) => {
        const [age, sex, subgroup] = k.split("|");
        const g = cases.filter((c) => band(c) === age && c.sex === sex && c.subgroup === subgroup);
        return {
          label: `${age} · ${sex} · ${subgroup}`,
          n: g.length,
          escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
          readmitRate: rate(g, (c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0)),
        };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate)
      .slice(0, 12);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's age, sex, subgroup, timeToEscalationDays, and readmitted90d.",
    process: "Cross-tabs age band (45–64, 65–74, 75+) × sex × subgroup, keeps strata with at least 5 cases, computes escalation and readmission rates per stratum, and returns the 12 worst by escalation.",
    output: "rows: one per stratum with {label, n, escRate, readmitRate} — the three-way stratification view.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "subgroup is a coded proxy (Subgroup A–D), not a protected class — reading rows as equity mislabels them.",
      "Age band feeds escProb; escRate/readmitRate are modeled — the worst-12 selection is a winner's curse over small n≥5 strata.",
      "Slice(0,12) by escalation means exactly the noisiest cells surface.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Three-way cells of modeled outcomes over proxy strata — nothing here survives as evidence.",
    ],
  },
};
Seasonal pattern

Groups patients by season of admission and reports how the mix of conditions and the escalation rate change with the calendar.

 {
  id: "seasonal-pattern",
  name: "Seasonal pattern",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Category mix and escalation rate by season (winter = Dec–Feb), to see whether the cohort's composition shifts with the calendar.",
  requiredFields: ["admitMonth", "category", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const seasonOf = (m: number) =>
      m === 12 || m <= 2 ? "Winter" : m <= 5 ? "Spring" : m <= 8 ? "Summer" : "Fall";
    const seasons = ["Winter", "Spring", "Summer", "Fall"];
    const rows = seasons.map((s) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => seasonOf(c.admitMonth) === s);
      const byCat = groupBy(g, (c) => c.category);
      const mix = [...byCat.entries()]
        .map(([cat, grp]) => ({ cat, share: g.length ? grp.length / g.length : 0 }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.share - a.share);
      return {
        label: s,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
        topCategory: mix[0]?.cat ?? "—",
        topShare: mix[0]?.share ?? 0,
      };
    });
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's admitMonth, category, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Groups cases by season derived from admitMonth (Winter = Dec–Feb, then Spring/Summer/Fall), and per season counts cases, computes escalation rate, and reports the most common category and its share.",
    output: "rows: one per season with {label, n, escRate, topCategory, topShare} — whether cohort composition shifts with the calendar.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "admitMonth is a uniform randInt(1,12) — any season gradient is sampling noise, not epidemiology.",
      "One admission snapshot backs every season; no longitudinal follow-up exists.",
      "topCategory share in small seasons flips with a handful of cases.",
    ],
  },
};

 {
  id: "adherence-sdoh",

        label: b,

        unflaggedRate: rate(unflagged, (c) => (c.guidelineAdherent ? 1 : 0)),
      };
    });
    return { rows, unflaggedN: unflagged.length };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's sdohBarriers and guidelineAdherent.",
    process: "For each distinct SDOH barrier, computes guideline-adherence among patients carrying it and among patients with no barriers at all, for direct contrast.",
    output: "rows: one per barrier with {label, n, adherentRate, unflaggedRate}, plus unflaggedN — separates 'won't take' from 'can't get'.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "guidelineAdherent is generated conditioned on the medication-cost barrier (synthetic.ts) — the barrier-vs-unflagged contrast is injected.",
      "Patients count once per barrier carried — multi-barrier patients appear in several rows.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The exposure (medication-cost barrier) sets the 'outcome' (adherence) in the generator.",
    ],
  },

 {

    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },

    input: "Each case's category, screeningUpToDate, timeToEscalationDays, and readmitted90d.",

    output: "rows: one per category with {label, n, escDue, escUptodate, readmitDue, readmitUptodate} — the due-vs-up-to-date outcome gap.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "screeningUpToDate is a modeled flag correlated with generator inputs; escalation and readmission are modeled — the due-vs-current gap is planted.",
      "Due/uptodate arms have no minimum n — small-category rates can rest on one or two patients.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The stratifier and outcomes are linked inside the generator.",
    ],
  },

    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };

  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "vaccinated is generated from the stratifier's correlates: sdohBarriers (synthetic.ts) or age>65 (fhir.ts) — escVax/escUnvax is confounded by construction.",

    ],

Symptom → investigation

For each common symptom, checks how often any follow-up test was ordered at all — a scan for missed work-ups, worst first.

 {
  id: "symptom-investigation",
  name: "Symptom → investigation",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "For each common symptom, how often the work-ups that should follow it actually get ordered — the missed-work-up scan.",
  requiredFields: ["symptoms", "investigations"],
  run(cases) {
    const counts = new Map<string, { n: number; anyInvest: number }>();
    for (const c of cases) {
      for (const s of c.symptoms) {
        let rec = counts.get(s);
        if (!rec) {
          rec = { n: 0, anyInvest: 0 };
          counts.set(s, rec);
        }
        rec.n++;
        if (c.investigations.length > 0) rec.anyInvest++;
      }
    }
    const rows = [...counts.entries()]
      .filter(([, r]) => r.n >= 15)
      .map(([sym, r]) => ({
        label: sym,
        n: r.n,
        investigatedRate: r.anyInvest / r.n,
      }))
      .sort((a, b) => a.investigatedRate - b.investigatedRate);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's symptoms and investigations.",
    process: "For each symptom seen in at least 15 cases, computes the share of those patients who received any investigation at all; sorted lowest rate first.",
    output: "rows: {label, n, investigatedRate} — the missed-work-up scan: symptoms whose work-ups often never get ordered.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "investigations were hardcoded placeholder strings until recently — on pre-import cohorts investigatedRate measures the placeholder.",
      "'Any investigation at all' is case-level, credited to every symptom the patient has — ecological fallacy within cases.",
      "Symptoms under n=15 vanish silently; the remaining rates have no expected-work-up denominator.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Unreliable input plus case-level attribution — the 'missed work-up' reading is unsafe.",
    ],
  },
};

 {

  requiredFields: ["losDays"],

    const bands: Array<[number, number, string]> = [
      [0, 3, "0–3d"], [4, 7, "4–7d"], [8, 14, "8–14d"], [15, 21, "15–21d"], [22, 999, "22d+"],

    const rows = bands.map(([lo, hi, label]) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => c.losDays >= lo && c.losDays <= hi);

    });

  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's losDays.",
    process: "Bins stays into five length-of-stay bands (0–3, 4–7, 8–14, 15–21, 22+ days) and counts cases and cohort share in each, plus the overall median.",
    output: "rows: one per band with {label, n, share}, plus top-level median — the full LOS histogram, not just the average.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "losDays is randInt(1,21) (or encounter duration in loaders) — the histogram describes the generator's draw, not throughput.",
      "Descriptive-only; safe as a plumbing check, misleading as an operations baseline.",
    ],
  },
};
Test-ordering intensity

Groups admissions by how many tests were ordered (0–2, 3–4, 5+) and checks whether heavier testing tracks sicker patients or just pads the stay.

 {
  id: "test-intensity",
  name: "Test-ordering intensity",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Investigations ordered per admission, banded: does test volume track acuity (comorbidity burden) or pad the stay?",
  requiredFields: ["investigations", "comorbidities", "losDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const bands: Array<[number, number, string]> = [
      [0, 2, "0–2 tests"], [3, 4, "3–4 tests"], [5, 99, "5+ tests"],
    ];
    const rate = (g: Case[], f: (c: Case) => number) => (g.length ? g.reduce((s, c) => s + f(c), 0) / g.length : 0);
    const rows = bands.map(([lo, hi, label]) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => c.investigations.length >= lo && c.investigations.length <= hi);
      return {
        label,
        n: g.length,
        avgComorbidities: rate(g, (c) => c.comorbidities.length),
        avgLos: rate(g, (c) => c.losDays),
        escRate: rate(g, (c) => (escalated(c) ? 1 : 0)),
      };
    }).filter((r) => r.n > 0);
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's investigations, comorbidities, losDays, and escalation outcome.",
    process: "Bands cases by number of investigations (0–2, 3–4, 5+), then averages comorbidity count and LOS and computes the escalation rate within each band; empty bands are dropped.",
    output: "rows: one per band with {label, n, avgComorbidities, avgLos, escRate} — read as whether test volume tracks acuity or pads the stay.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "investigations were hardcoded placeholder strings until recently — on pre-import cohorts the bands measure the placeholder, not orders.",
      "escRate by test band is circular: comorbidity burden drives both test volume correlations and the modeled escalation formula.",
      "avgLos comparison invites 'tests pad the stay' reading — losDays is randInt(1,21), independent of investigations.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Investigation counts are unreliable on legacy cohorts, and the escalation contrast is injected.",
    ],
  },
};
Checkpoint paths

Sorts patients by when — or whether — they got worse (early, mid, late, never) and describes each path's typical stay and discharge outcome.

 {
  id: "checkpoint-paths",
  name: "Checkpoint paths",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "How cases traverse the escalation checkpoint of the 6-checkpoint continuum: early (≤3d), mid (4–9d), late (≥10d), or never — with disposition mix per path.",
  requiredFields: ["timeToEscalationDays", "disposition", "checkpoints"],
  run(cases) {
    const pathOf = (c: Case) => {
      const t = c.timeToEscalationDays;
      if (t === null) return "no escalation";
      if (t <= 3) return "early (≤3d)";
      if (t <= 9) return "mid (4–9d)";
      return "late (≥10d)";
    };
    const paths = ["early (≤3d)", "mid (4–9d)", "late (≥10d)", "no escalation"];
    const rows = paths.map((p) => {
      const g = cases.filter((c) => pathOf(c) === p);
      return {
        label: p,
        n: g.length,
        share: g.length / cases.length,
        medianLos: median(g.map((c) => c.losDays)),
        homePct: g.length ? g.filter((c) => c.disposition === "home").length / g.length : 0,
      };
    });
    void groupBy;
    return { rows };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's timeToEscalationDays, disposition, and losDays.",
    process: "Classifies each case as escalating early (≤3d), mid (4–9d), late (≥10d), or never, then counts each path and computes median LOS and share discharged home within it.",
    output: "rows: one per path with {label, n, share, medianLos, homePct} — read as how escalation timing shapes where patients end up.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "timeToEscalationDays and disposition are both generator outputs — 'how escalation timing shapes disposition' compares two formulas.",
      "Path bands (≤3/4–9/≥10d) slice the modeled tte ranges (1–5 injected, 1–14 else) — bands align with generator draws, not clinical phases.",
      "Single snapshot: paths are co-occurrence of two modeled variables, not an observed sequence.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Both the path and its outcomes are modeled from the same generator formulas.",
    ],
  },
};
Hub network

Maps which conditions connect to which categories, then ranks conditions by how many different categories they appear in — the 'hub' conditions.

 {
  id: "hub-network",
  name: "Hub network",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Category ↔ comorbidity edges occurring in at least 8 cases, with each comorbidity's hub degree — how many categories it connects to.",
  requiredFields: ["category", "comorbidities"],
  run(cases) {
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    const edges: Array<{ cat: string; com: string; n: number }> = [];
    const comDegree = new Map<string, number>();
    const coms = [...new Set(cases.flatMap((c) => c.comorbidities))].sort();
    byCat.forEach((group, cat) => {
      for (const com of coms) {
        const n = group.filter((c) => c.comorbidities.includes(com)).length;
        if (n >= MIN_EDGE) {
          edges.push({ cat, com, n });
          comDegree.set(com, (comDegree.get(com) ?? 0) + 1);
        }
      }
    });
    const rows = edges
      .map((e) => ({ label: `${e.com} → ${e.cat}`, n: e.n, degree: comDegree.get(e.com) ?? 0 }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.degree - a.degree || b.n - a.n);
    return { rows, nEdges: rows.length };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's category and comorbidities.",
    process: "For every category × comorbidity edge occurring in at least 8 cases, keeps the edge and increments the comorbidity's hub degree — how many categories it connects to; ranked by degree then volume.",
    output: "rows: {label ('comorbidity → category'), n, degree}, plus nEdges — high-degree comorbidities are the cohort's connective tissue.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "ok",
    points: [
      "MIN_EDGE=8 is a volume floor, not a test — hub degree scales with cohort size and category size, not clinical connectivity.",
      "Multi-coded cases count toward every edge — edges share patients, so degrees are not independent.",
    ],
  },
};
Cohort overview

The master summary: counts patients, splits by sex and category, computes the headline baselines (age, escalation, readmission, stay length), lists the most common conditions, symptoms and medications, and the pairs that travel together. Every other analytic is read against this reference frame.

 {
  id: "cohort-overview",
  name: "Cohort overview",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "The full picture of the cohort: size, demographics, clinical makeup (comorbidities, symptoms, medications, investigations, procedures — coded where present) and outcome baselines — the reference frame every other analytic is read against.",
  requiredFields: ["age", "sex"],
  run(cases) {
    const byCat = groupBy(cases, (c) => c.category);
    const total = Math.max(1, cases.length);
    // ── distributions that drive the on-screen panels ──

    const polyph = [0, 0, 0, 0]; // 0, 1-2, 3-4, 5+

    for (const c of cases) {
      const k = c.polypharmacyCount;
      if (k === 0) polyph[0]++;
      else if (k <= 2) polyph[1]++;
      else if (k <= 4) polyph[2]++;
      else polyph[3]++;
    }
    const dispOrder = ["home", "step-down", "rehab", "expired"] as const;
    const dispCounts = new Map<string, number>(dispOrder.map((d) => [d, 0]));
    for (const c of cases) dispCounts.set(c.disposition, (dispCounts.get(c.disposition) ?? 0) + 1);
    const ageBuckets = [0, 0, 0, 0, 0];
    for (const c of cases) {
      const a = c.age;
      if (a < 18) ageBuckets[0]++;
      else if (a < 45) ageBuckets[1]++;
      else if (a < 65) ageBuckets[2]++;
      else if (a < 80) ageBuckets[3]++;
      else ageBuckets[4]++;
    }
    const encBuckets = [0, 0, 0, 0]; // 0, 1-2, 3-5, 6+

    for (const c of cases) {
      const k = c.encounters90d;
      if (k === 0) encBuckets[0]++;
      else if (k <= 2) encBuckets[1]++;
      else if (k <= 5) encBuckets[2]++;
      else encBuckets[3]++;
    }
    const escByCat = [...byCat.entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => ({
        label: cat,
        value: g.length ? g.filter(escalated).length / g.length : 0,
        n: g.length,
      }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.value - a.value);

    return {
      rows: [
        { label: "Patients", value: cases.length },
        { label: "Mean age (years)", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.age)) },
        { label: "Female", value: cases.filter((c) => c.sex === "F").length },
        { label: "Male", value: cases.filter((c) => c.sex === "M").length },
        ...[...byCat.entries()]
          .sort((a, b) => b[1].length - a[1].length)
          .map(([cat, g]) => ({ label: `Category: ${cat}`, value: g.length })),
        { label: "Escalation rate", value: cases.filter(escalated).length / total },
        { label: "90-day readmission rate", value: cases.filter((c) => c.readmitted90d).length / total },
        { label: "Median LOS (days)", value: median(cases.map((c) => c.losDays)) },
        { label: "Mean 90-day encounters", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.encounters90d)) },
        { label: "With any SDOH barrier", value: cases.filter((c) => c.sdohBarriers.length > 0).length },
        { label: "Up to date on screening", value: cases.filter((c) => c.screeningUpToDate).length },
        { label: "Vaccinated", value: cases.filter((c) => c.vaccinated).length },
        { label: "Mean comorbidities per patient", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.comorbidities.length)) },
        { label: "Mean medications per patient", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.medications.length)) },
        { label: "Mean symptoms per patient", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.symptoms.length)) },
        { label: "Mean investigations per patient", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.investigations.length)) },
        { label: "Mean procedures per patient", value: mean(cases.map((c) => c.procedures.length)) },
      ],
      byComorbidity: topNWithIds(cases, (c) => c.comorbidityCodes, (c) => c.comorbidities, 8),
      bySymptom: topNWithIds(cases, (c) => c.symptomCodes, (c) => c.symptoms, 8),
      byMedication: topNWithIds(cases, (c) => c.medicationCodes, (c) => c.medications, 8),
      byInvestigation: topNWithIds(cases, (c) => c.observationCodes, (c) => c.investigations, 5),
      byProcedure: topNWithIds(cases, (c) => c.procedureCodes, (c) => c.procedures, 8),
      coOccurrenceComorbidity: topCoOccurrenceWithIds(cases, (c) => c.comorbidityCodes, (c) => c.comorbidities, 10),
      coOccurrenceInvestigation: topCoOccurrenceWithIds(cases, (c) => c.observationCodes, (c) => c.investigations, 10),
      coOccurrenceProcedure: topCoOccurrenceWithIds(cases, (c) => c.procedureCodes, (c) => c.procedures, 10),
      polypharmacy: [
        { label: "0 meds", value: polyph[0] },
        { label: "1–2 meds", value: polyph[1] },
        { label: "3–4 meds", value: polyph[2] },
        { label: "5+ meds", value: polyph[3] },
      ],
      disposition: dispOrder.map((d) => ({ label: d, value: dispCounts.get(d) ?? 0 })),
      ageBands: [
        { label: "0–17", value: ageBuckets[0] },
        { label: "18–44", value: ageBuckets[1] },
        { label: "45–64", value: ageBuckets[2] },
        { label: "65–79", value: ageBuckets[3] },
        { label: "80+", value: ageBuckets[4] },
      ],
      encounterBands: [
        { label: "0 encounters", value: encBuckets[0] },
        { label: "1–2", value: encBuckets[1] },
        { label: "3–5", value: encBuckets[2] },
        { label: "6+", value: encBuckets[3] },
      ],
      escByCat,
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The whole cohort; only age, sex, and standard outcome fields (losDays, readmitted90d, encounters90d, sdohBarriers, screeningUpToDate, vaccinated) are read.",
    process: "Counts patients, splits by sex and category, and computes cohort-wide baselines: mean age, escalation rate, 90-day readmission rate, median LOS, mean encounters, SDOH/screening/vaccination counts.",
    output: "rows: one label/value pair per metric (e.g. 'Escalation rate', 'Median LOS (days)') — the reference frame every other analytic is read against.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Escalation/readmission baselines are generator formulas — read them as simulation parameters, not cohort findings.",
      "90-day labels (readmitted90d, encounters90d) ride a single admission snapshot with no real follow-up behind them.",
    ],
  },
};
Index case comparison

Picks one patient and shows where they stand compared to the whole cohort — each of their numbers is ranked as a percentile against everyone else.

 {
  id: "index-case",
  name: "Index case comparison",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "One patient's whole trajectory — LOS, burden, utilization, risk — read against the cohort benchmark in percentiles. Pick a case id in the panel; it defaults to the first case.",
  requiredFields: ["losDays", "encounters90d", "comorbidities", "polypharmacyCount", "age"],
  run(cases, params) {
    cases = diseaseFilter(cases, params?.disease);
    const c = params?.caseId
      ? cases.find((x) => x.id === params.caseId) ?? cases.find((x) => x.id.toLowerCase() === params.caseId!.toLowerCase())
      : cases[0];
    if (!c) return { error: `case '${params?.caseId}' not in cohort`, caseId: params?.caseId ?? null };
    // scope: whole cohort (default) or the case's own category as the benchmark pool

    const scope = params?.scope === "category" ? "category" : "cohort";
    const benchmark = scope === "category" ? cases.filter((x) => x.category === c.category) : cases;
    const rows = [
      {
        metric: "Age",
        value: c.age,
        median: median(benchmark.map((x) => x.age)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map((x) => x.age), c.age),
      },
      {
        metric: "Comorbidity count",
        value: c.comorbidities.length,
        median: median(benchmark.map((x) => x.comorbidities.length)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map((x) => x.comorbidities.length), c.comorbidities.length),
      },
      {
        metric: "Active medications",
        value: c.polypharmacyCount,
        median: median(benchmark.map((x) => x.polypharmacyCount)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map((x) => x.polypharmacyCount), c.polypharmacyCount),
      },
      {
        metric: "Length of stay (days)",
        value: c.losDays,
        median: median(benchmark.map((x) => x.losDays)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map((x) => x.losDays), c.losDays),
      },
      {
        metric: "90-day encounters",
        value: c.encounters90d,
        median: median(benchmark.map((x) => x.encounters90d)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map((x) => x.encounters90d), c.encounters90d),
      },
      {
        metric: "Risk score",
        value: riskOf(c),
        median: median(benchmark.map(riskOf)) ?? 0,
        percentile: percentileRank(benchmark.map(riskOf), riskOf(c)),
      },
    ];
    return {
      scope,
      case: {
        id: c.id,
        category: c.category,
        age: c.age,
        sex: c.sex,
        comorbidities: c.comorbidities,
        disposition: c.disposition,
        escalated: escalated(c),
        sdohBarriers: c.sdohBarriers,
        checkpoints: c.checkpoints.map((cp) => ({ name: cp.name, value: cp.value, day: cp.day })),
      },
      rows,
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Params caseId (defaults to the first case) and optional disease filter and scope; per-case age, comorbidities, polypharmacyCount, losDays, encounters90d.",
    process: "Finds the selected patient, computes a risk score (age/10 + comorbidity count + 1 if 5+ meds), and reports each metric's value, cohort (or category) median, and percentile rank against that benchmark.",
    output: "case (id, checkpoints, flags) plus rows: one per metric with {metric, value, median, percentile} — read percentiles as the patient's standing vs the benchmark.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "riskOf (age/10 + comorbidities + polypharmacy bonus) repeats escProb's terms — a 'high-risk' percentile patient was made high-risk by the generator.",
      "encounters90d benchmarks a modeled draw — a real patient's percentile against it is meaningless.",
      "Percentiles are unadjusted cohort or category pools — case-mix differences read as patient differences.",
    ],
  },
};

export const trajectoryMap: Analytic = {
  id: "trajectory-map",
  name: "Trajectory map",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "Every patient walked through the same six checkpoints. Each node is a distinct checkpoint state with height = headcount; every ribbon is one group's transition between consecutive checkpoints — skipped steps and converging paths become visible at a glance.",
  requiredFields: ["checkpoints"],
  run(cases, params) {
    cases = diseaseFilter(cases, params?.disease);
    const NAMES = ["Diagnosis", "Presentation", "Initial plan", "Escalation", "Advanced/recurrent", "Disposition"];
    const valueOf = (c: Case, name: string) =>
      c.checkpoints.find((cp) => cp.name === name)?.value ?? "(none)";
    const stages = NAMES.map((name) => {
      const counts = new Map<string, number>();
      cases.forEach((c) => {
        const v = valueOf(c, name);
        counts.set(v, (counts.get(v) ?? 0) + 1);
      });
      const rows = [...counts.entries()]
        .map(([value, n]) => ({ value, n, pct: n / cases.length }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
      return { label: name, rows };
    });
    // edges: every group's transition between consecutive checkpoints

    const edges = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < NAMES.length - 1; i++) {
      const flows = new Map<string, number>();
      cases.forEach((c) => {
        const k = `${valueOf(c, NAMES[i])}|${valueOf(c, NAMES[i + 1])}`;
        flows.set(k, (flows.get(k) ?? 0) + 1);
      });
      edges.push({
        from: NAMES[i],
        to: NAMES[i + 1],
        flows: [...flows.entries()]
          .map(([k, n]) => { const [a, b] = k.split("|"); return { a, b, n }; })
          .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n),
      });
    }
    // optional single-patient overlay (the index case, when provided)

    const highlightId = params?.highlightCaseId;
    const highlight = highlightId
      ? cases.find((c) => c.id === highlightId)?.checkpoints.map((cp) => cp.value) ?? null
      : null;
    return { stages, edges, highlight, highlightCaseId: highlightId ?? null, total: cases.length };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withCp = cases.filter((c) => c.checkpoints.length > 0).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withCp / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`cases with checkpoints: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's checkpoints (Diagnosis, Presentation, Initial plan, Escalation, Advanced/recurrent, Disposition); params disease and highlightCaseId.",
    process: "Counts the distinct value distribution at each of the six checkpoints, then tallies every patient's from→to transition between consecutive checkpoints; optionally overlays one patient's path.",
    output: "stages: {label, rows:[{value, n, pct}]}, edges: per-hop flows {a, b, n}, highlight, total — each ribbon is one group's real path through the continuum.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Checkpoint values are assigned by deriveCheckpoints from the same tte draw — ribbons re-express one modeled variable, not observed flow.",
      "Missing checkpoint values fall into '(none)' but the stage denominator stays the whole cohort — pct rows can under-run 1.",
      "Single snapshot: edges are state co-occurrence, not verified sequence.",
    ],
  },
};

  requiredFields: [],
  run(cases, params) {

        { label: "Male", value: cases.filter((c) => c.sex === "M").length },

        { label: "90-day readmission rate", value: mean(cases.map((c) => (c.readmitted90d ? 1 : 0))) },

      ],
    };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Params disease (comma-separated disease ids); per-case age, sex, comorbidities, sdohBarriers, and outcome fields among the filtered sub-cohort.",
    process: "Filters the cohort to patients carrying the selected disease(s), then computes the same baselines as the cohort overview: size, demographics, escalation, readmission, median LOS, encounters, SDOH, comorbidity mean.",
    output: "rows: one label/value pair per metric — the disease sub-cohort's reference frame.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Outcome baselines are modeled formulas computed on the disease-filtered slice — small sub-cohorts make them noisy simulation parameters.",
      "Disease filter with unknown ids silently contributes nothing — a typo'd disease param returns the full cohort instead.",
    ],
  },

Trajectory map

Draws every patient's journey through fixed stages of care as a flow diagram: node height is how many patients are in each state, ribbons are groups moving between stages, and one patient's own path can be overlaid.

 {
  id: "trajectory-map",
  name: "Trajectory map",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "Every patient walked through the same six checkpoints. Each node is a distinct checkpoint state with height = headcount; every ribbon is one group's transition between consecutive checkpoints — skipped steps and converging paths become visible at a glance.",
  requiredFields: ["checkpoints"],
  run(cases, params) {
    cases = diseaseFilter(cases, params?.disease);
    const NAMES = ["Diagnosis", "Presentation", "Initial plan", "Escalation", "Advanced/recurrent", "Disposition"];
    const valueOf = (c: Case, name: string) =>
      c.checkpoints.find((cp) => cp.name === name)?.value ?? "(none)";
    const stages = NAMES.map((name) => {
      const counts = new Map<string, number>();
      cases.forEach((c) => {
        const v = valueOf(c, name);
        counts.set(v, (counts.get(v) ?? 0) + 1);
      });
      const rows = [...counts.entries()]
        .map(([value, n]) => ({ value, n, pct: n / cases.length }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
      return { label: name, rows };
    });
    // edges: every group's transition between consecutive checkpoints

    const edges = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < NAMES.length - 1; i++) {
      const flows = new Map<string, number>();
      cases.forEach((c) => {
        const k = `${valueOf(c, NAMES[i])}|${valueOf(c, NAMES[i + 1])}`;
        flows.set(k, (flows.get(k) ?? 0) + 1);
      });
      edges.push({
        from: NAMES[i],
        to: NAMES[i + 1],
        flows: [...flows.entries()]
          .map(([k, n]) => { const [a, b] = k.split("|"); return { a, b, n }; })
          .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n),
      });
    }
    // optional single-patient overlay (the index case, when provided)

    const highlightId = params?.highlightCaseId;
    const highlight = highlightId
      ? cases.find((c) => c.id === highlightId)?.checkpoints.map((cp) => cp.value) ?? null
      : null;
    return { stages, edges, highlight, highlightCaseId: highlightId ?? null, total: cases.length };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withCp = cases.filter((c) => c.checkpoints.length > 0).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withCp / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`cases with checkpoints: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's checkpoints (Diagnosis, Presentation, Initial plan, Escalation, Advanced/recurrent, Disposition); params disease and highlightCaseId.",
    process: "Counts the distinct value distribution at each of the six checkpoints, then tallies every patient's from→to transition between consecutive checkpoints; optionally overlays one patient's path.",
    output: "stages: {label, rows:[{value, n, pct}]}, edges: per-hop flows {a, b, n}, highlight, total — each ribbon is one group's real path through the continuum.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Checkpoint values are assigned by deriveCheckpoints from the same tte draw — ribbons re-express one modeled variable, not observed flow.",
      "Missing checkpoint values fall into '(none)' but the stage denominator stays the whole cohort — pct rows can under-run 1.",
      "Single snapshot: edges are state co-occurrence, not verified sequence.",
    ],
  },
};

    void cases;
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Params disease (comma-separated disease ids); per-case age, sex, comorbidities, sdohBarriers, and outcome fields among the filtered sub-cohort.",
    process: "Filters the cohort to patients carrying the selected disease(s), then computes the same baselines as the cohort overview: size, demographics, escalation, readmission, median LOS, encounters, SDOH, comorbidity mean.",
    output: "rows: one label/value pair per metric — the disease sub-cohort's reference frame.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Outcome baselines are modeled formulas computed on the disease-filtered slice — small sub-cohorts make them noisy simulation parameters.",
      "Disease filter with unknown ids silently contributes nothing — a typo'd disease param returns the full cohort instead.",
    ],
  },
};
Transition matrix

For each step of the care path, reports the probability of going from every state to every next state — a forecast of where patients typically go next.

 {
  id: "transition-matrix",
  name: "Transition matrix",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "Markov-chain style stage-to-stage transition probabilities: for each of the five checkpoint hops, every observed from-state's complete distribution over next-states across all patients — with a weighted top-transition probability per hop summarizing how deterministic that hop is.",
  requiredFields: ["checkpoints"],
  run(cases) {
    const valueOf = (c: Case, name: string) =>
      c.checkpoints.find((cp) => cp.name === name)?.value ?? null;

    const hops: TransitionHop[] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < STAGES.length - 1; i++) {
      const fromName = STAGES[i];
      const toName = STAGES[i + 1];
      const toCounts = new Map<string, Map<string, number>>();
      const fromTotals = new Map<string, number>();
      cases.forEach((c) => {
        const fromV = valueOf(c, fromName);
        const toV = valueOf(c, toName);
        if (!fromV || !toV) return;
        if (!toCounts.has(fromV)) toCounts.set(fromV, new Map());
        const m = toCounts.get(fromV)!;
        m.set(toV, (m.get(toV) ?? 0) + 1);
        fromTotals.set(fromV, (fromTotals.get(fromV) ?? 0) + 1);
      });
      const rows: TransitionRow[] = [...toCounts.entries()]
        .map(([fromV, m]) => {
          const total = fromTotals.get(fromV) ?? 0;
          const dist = [...m.entries()]
            .map(([toV, n]) => ({ to: toV, n, prob: total ? n / total : 0 }))
            .sort((a, b) => b.prob - a.prob || b.n - a.n);
          return { from: fromV, n: total, dist, top: dist[0] ? { to: dist[0].to, prob: dist[0].prob } : null };
        })
        .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
      const totalN = rows.reduce((a, r) => a + r.n, 0);
      const weightedTopProb = totalN
        ? rows.reduce((a, r) => a + (r.top?.prob ?? 0) * r.n, 0) / totalN
        : 0;
      hops.push({ from: fromName, to: toName, rows, totalN, weightedTopProb });
    }
    return { hops };
  },
  quality(cases) {
    const withCp = cases.filter((c) => c.checkpoints.length > 0).length;
    const pct = cases.length ? withCp / cases.length : 0;
    if (pct < 0.2) return { level: "weak", gaps: [`cases with checkpoints: ${Math.round(pct * 100)}%`] };
    return { level: "good", gaps: [] };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each case's checkpoints (Diagnosis, Presentation, Initial plan, Escalation, Advanced/recurrent, Disposition).",
    process: "For each of the five checkpoint hops, counts every observed from-state's distribution over next-states and converts counts to probabilities; also computes a headcount-weighted mean of each hop's top-transition probability.",
    output: "hops: {from, to, rows [{from, n, dist [{to, n, prob}], top}], totalN, weightedTopProb} — the full Markov-style matrix; weightedTopProb near 1 means a deterministic hop.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Checkpoint values derive from one tte draw in deriveCheckpoints — 'transition probabilities' re-express that draw, not observed dynamics.",
      "Single admission snapshot with no time axis: Markov language overstates what are co-occurrence counts.",
      "Rows with tiny n still report probabilities to three decimals — weightedTopProb smooths over unstable hops.",
    ],
  },
};
Medication co-prescription (Coded)

Same as Medication co-prescription, but pairs drugs by their standard RxNorm code instead of their name — so a drug listed under different names still counts as one drug.

 {

  requiredFields: ["medications", "medicationCodes", "timeToEscalationDays"],
  run(cases) {
    const rows = topPairs(cases, medKey, 6, 10).map((r) => ({

      return { level: "weak", gaps: [`medicationCodes populated for only ${Math.round(pct * 100)}% of cohort; pairs fall back to strings for the rest`] };

    input: "Each case's medicationCodes (RxNorm) when available, else medications string list, plus escalation outcome.",

  },

    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Pair members feed polypharmacyCount, an escProb term — pair escalation rates are partly arithmetic identity.",
      "Patients count once per pair carried — pair ns overlap and overstate precision.",
      "min 6 patients per pair but no minimum on any other cell; escRate has no uncertainty.",
      "When medicationCodes is sparse, this analytic silently mixes code-keyed and string-keyed pairs in the same table.",
