      "avgLos comparison invites a 'tests pad the stay' reading, but episode LOS is date arithmetic over generated events.",
      "Labs dated by the generator, not order entry — band counts measure the generator's intensity parameter.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Test-volume bands and outcomes come from the same generator formulas — the contrast is injected.",
    ],
  },
};
Polypharmacy (concurrent overlap)

Instead of counting drugs on a list, it sweeps each patient's medication timeline day by day to find how many drugs overlapped at once, then reports outcomes by peak overlap — true concurrent polypharmacy.

 {
  id: "polypharmacy",
  name: "Polypharmacy (concurrent overlap)",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Longitudinal concurrent-medication overlap from exposure windows: patients bucketed by peak simultaneous medications, with med-days and modeled escalation/readmission rates per bucket.",
  requiredCategories: ["medication", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const overlap = overlapByPatient(events);
    const buckets: Array<[number, number, string]> = [
      [0, 1, "0–1 concurrent"], [2, 3, "2–3 concurrent"],
      [4, 5, "4–5 concurrent"], [6, 99, "6+ concurrent"],
    ];
    const rows = buckets.map(([lo, hi, label]) => {
      const g = views.filter((v) => {
        const s = overlap.get(v.patientId);
        return s && s.maxConcurrent >= lo && s.maxConcurrent <= hi;
      });
      return {
        label,
        n: g.length,
        meanMedDays: r2(mean(g, (v) => overlap.get(v.patientId)?.medDays ?? 0)),
        meanCourses: r2(mean(g, (v) => overlap.get(v.patientId)?.courses ?? 0)),
        ...outcomeRates(g),
      };
    }).filter((r) => r.n > 0);
    return { rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's medication events with course windows (exposureWindows), plus modeled escalation and 90-day readmission outcome events.",
    process: "Sweeps each patient's medication windows day by day to get peak concurrent medications and total overlapping med-days within the observation period, buckets patients by peak concurrency, and computes mean med-days, course count, and outcome rates per bucket.",
    output: "rows: one per concurrency bucket with {label, n, meanMedDays, meanCourses, escRate, readmitRate} — burden of simultaneous therapy first, outcomes second.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Concurrency buckets and med-days are genuinely longitudinal — computed from exposure windows, not the admission count field.",
      "Outcome events are MODELED by the generator; polypharmacyCount is an escProb/readmitProb term, so the per-bucket gradient is injected.",
      "Ongoing courses are extended to the observation end — an artificial worst case if prescribers simply never recorded stop dates.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The bucket variable (medication burden) is a coefficient of the modeled outcomes — bucket outcome rates are circular.",
    ],
  },
};
Medication co-prescription

The same co-prescription pairs, now counted once per patient from dated medication events.

 {
  id: "med-combos",
  name: "Medication co-prescription",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "The most co-prescribed medication pairs, each pair counted once per patient carrying both, with the modeled escalation rate among those patients.",
  requiredCategories: ["medication", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const pairs = new Map<string, { a: string; b: string; n: number; esc: number }>();
    for (const v of views) {
      const meds = [...new Set(v.events
        .filter((e) => e.category === "medication")
        .map((e) => e.code || e.display.toLowerCase()))]
        .map((k) => {
          const ev = v.events.find((e) => e.category === "medication" && (e.code || e.display.toLowerCase()) === k);
          return ev?.display || k;
        });
      for (let i = 0; i < meds.length; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < meds.length; j++) {
          const [a, b] = [meds[i], meds[j]].sort();
          const key = `${a}|${b}`;
          const rec = pairs.get(key) ?? { a, b, n: 0, esc: 0 };
          rec.n++;
          if (v.escalated) rec.esc++;
          pairs.set(key, rec);
        }
      }
    }
    const rows = [...pairs.values()]
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 6)
      .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n)
      .slice(0, 10)
      .map((r) => ({ label: `${r.a} + ${r.b}`, n: r.n, escRate: r2(r.esc / r.n) }));
    return { rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's distinct medication events and the modeled escalation outcome event.",
    process: "Tallies every co-prescribed medication pair within a patient (once per patient), keeps pairs carried by at least 6 patients, and computes the escalation rate among patients carrying each pair; top 10 by volume.",
    output: "rows: {label ('A + B'), n, escRate} — the pair-level co-prescription landscape with an associational outcome overlay.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Pair members feed polypharmacyCount, an escProb term — pair escalation rates are partly arithmetic identity.",
      "Escalation is a MODELED outcome event; the outcome overlay is generator-injected, not observed pharmacology.",
      "Pair ns overlap (patients count in many pairs) and no uncertainty accompanies any rate.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Exposure pairs tie into the modeled escalation formula via polypharmacy burden.",
    ],
  },
};
Pharmacovigilance

Looks only at patients on 5+ medications, reports how often each category gets worse, and flags medications that show up unusually often among the patients who got worse.

 {
  id: "pharmacovigilance",
  name: "Pharmacovigilance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Restricted to polypharmacy patients (5+ active medications): modeled escalation rates per category, plus the medications most over-represented among escalated versus non-escalated patients.",
  requiredCategories: ["medication", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const poly = views.filter((v) => (v.polypharmacyCount ?? 0) >= 5);
    const rows = [...groupBy(poly, (v) => v.category ?? "").entries()]
      .map(([cat, g]) => ({ cat, n: g.length, escRate: r2(rate(g, (v) => v.escalated === true)) }))
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 5)
      .sort((a, b) => b.escRate - a.escRate)
      .slice(0, 8);
    const esc = poly.filter((v) => v.escalated === true);
    const nonEsc = poly.filter((v) => v.escalated !== true);
    const medShare = (g: CaseView[]) => {
      const m = new Map<string, { display: string; n: number }>();
      for (const v of g) {
        const seen = new Set<string>();
        for (const e of v.events) {
          if (e.category !== "medication") continue;
          const key = e.code || e.display.toLowerCase();
          if (seen.has(key)) continue;
          seen.add(key);
          const rec = m.get(key) ?? { display: e.display || key, n: 0 };
          rec.n++;
          m.set(key, rec);
        }
      }
      return m;
    };
    const escMeds = medShare(esc);
    const nonEscMeds = medShare(nonEsc);
    const signals = [...escMeds.entries()]
      .map(([key, rec]) => ({
        med: rec.display,
        escShare: r2(rec.n / Math.max(esc.length, 1)),
        ratio: r2((rec.n / Math.max(esc.length, 1)) /
          Math.max((nonEscMeds.get(key)?.n ?? 0) / Math.max(nonEsc.length, 1), 0.01)),
      }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.ratio - a.ratio)
      .slice(0, 5);
    return { nPoly: poly.length, rows, signals };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's polypharmacyCount from the encounter attributes, medication events, category, and the modeled escalation outcome event.",
    process: "Restricts to patients with 5+ active medications, computes escalation rate per category (n ≥ 5, top 8), then compares each medication's share among escalated versus non-escalated polypharmacy patients as a ratio; top 5 ratios reported.",
    output: "nPoly, rows {cat, n, escRate}, and signals: top 5 {med, escShare, ratio} — ratio > 1 means over-representation among escalated cases (associational only).",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "The 5+ medication screen stratifies on an escProb term — escalation rates here are pre-injected by the generator.",
      "Escalation is a MODELED outcome event; medication 'signals' compare shares among modeled escalated vs not — ecological ratios with no patient-level matching.",
      "The ratio's denominator share is floored at 0.01, but a drug seen once among few non-escalated patients still explodes to the top.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Denominator (polypharmacy burden) is a cause of the modeled outcome — circular by construction.",
    ],
  },
};
Readmission drivers

The same lift scan for readmission, but over comorbidity events in the stream; the outcomes are the modeled outcome events, so read results with that caveat.

 {
  id: "readmission-drivers",
  name: "Readmission drivers",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Per diagnosis category, the comorbidity whose presence lifts the modeled 90-day readmission rate the most — backward-lift arithmetic pointed at readmission.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const rows: Array<{ cat: string; com: string; n: number; rWith: number; rWithout: number; lift: number }> = [];
    for (const [cat, group] of groupBy(views, (v) => v.category ?? "")) {
      const withSets = comorbiditySets(group);
      const allComs = [...new Set(withSets.flatMap((s) => s.coms))].filter((c) => c !== cat);
      let best: (typeof rows)[number] | null = null;
      for (const com of allComs) {
        const withCom = withSets.filter((s) => s.coms.includes(com)).map((s) => s.view);
        const withoutCom = withSets.filter((s) => !s.coms.includes(com)).map((s) => s.view);
        if (withCom.length < 4 || withoutCom.length < 2) continue;
        const rWith = rate(withCom, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true);
        const rWithout = rate(withoutCom, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true);
        const lift = rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : 0;
        if (!best || lift > best.lift) {
          best = { cat, com, n: withCom.length, rWith: r2(rWith), rWithout: r2(rWithout), lift: r2(lift) };
        }
      }
      if (best) rows.push(best);
    }
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.lift - a.lift).slice(0, 10) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's comorbidity events, diagnosis category, and the modeled 90-day readmission outcome event.",
    process: "Within each category, scans every observed comorbidity with at least 4 patients having it and 2 without, computes the readmission rate with vs without, and keeps the comorbidity with the highest lift (with ÷ without).",
    output: "rows: top 10 {cat, com, n, rWith, rWithout, lift} — the biggest per-category readmission lift, fully auditable from the two rates.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Readmission is a MODELED outcome event whose probability includes the comorbidity count — the winning comorbidity is the formula's own coefficient.",
      "Max-of-scan over observed comorbidities with min n 4/2 — winner's curse on small cells.",
      "lift collapses to 0 when rWithout is 0, silently hiding uncomputable rows instead of flagging them.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Comorbidity count is a direct input of the modeled readmission probability — lifts restate the generator.",
    ],
  },
};
Forward hypothesis

You name a category and a comorbidity; it finds the patients carrying both as dated events and compares their outcomes to same-category patients without it.

 {
  id: "forward-hypothesis",
  name: "Forward hypothesis",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Tests one stated hypothesis — a category plus a comorbidity you name via params — and reads off modeled escalation, readmission, and length-of-stay against the same-category baseline.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity", "outcome"],
  run(events, params) {
    const cat = params?.cat;
    const com = params?.com;
    if (!cat || !com) {
      return { error: "params 'cat' (category) and 'com' (comorbidity) are required" };
    }
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const comLower = com.toLowerCase();
    const hasCom = (v: CaseView) =>
      v.events.some((e) => e.category === "comorbidity" &&
        (e.code === com || e.display.toLowerCase().includes(comLower)));
    const cohort = views.filter((v) => v.category === cat && hasCom(v));
    const baseline = views.filter((v) => v.category === cat && !hasCom(v));
    return {
      pair: {
        cat,
        com,
        n: cohort.length,
        nBase: baseline.length,
        escRate: r2(rate(cohort, (v) => v.escalated === true)),
        escRateBase: r2(rate(baseline, (v) => v.escalated === true)),
        readmit: r2(rate(cohort, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true)),
        readmitBase: r2(rate(baseline, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true)),
        los: r2(mean(cohort, (v) => v.losDays ?? 0)),
        losBase: r2(mean(baseline, (v) => v.losDays ?? 0)),
      },
    };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Params cat (category) and com (comorbidity); each patient's category, comorbidity events, and modeled escalation/readmission outcome events plus encounter length-of-stay.",
    process: "Selects patients in the named category who carry the named comorbidity event, and reads off escalation, readmission, and mean LOS against same-category patients without it — a direct hypothesis test, no scanning or ranking.",
    output: "pair: {cat, com, n, nBase, escRate, escRateBase, readmit, readmitBase, los, losBase} — the stated hypothesis answered with cohort rates.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Comorbidities drive the modeled escalation and readmission probabilities — a 'confirmed' hypothesis restates the generator.",
      "Escalation and readmission are MODELED outcome events, stated here so the contrast is never read as observed care.",
      "No minimum n — a two-patient hypothesis can return a finite-looking rate contrast.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The tested exposure is an input to the modeled outcomes — confirmation is circular.",
    ],
  },
};
Backward factors

The same with-versus-without comparison for four fixed factors (age 65+, 3+ conditions, 5+ medications, ongoing medication course), computed from the event stream.

 {
  id: "backward-factors",
  name: "Backward factors",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Cohort-wide with/without escalation lift for a fixed set of coded factors: age 65+, 3+ comorbidity events, 5+ active medications, and an ongoing medication course at the last observed event.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity", "medication", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const overlap = overlapByPatient(events);
    const factors: Array<{ label: string; test: (v: CaseView) => boolean }> = [
      { label: "Age 65 or older", test: (v) => (v.age ?? 0) >= 65 },
      { label: "3+ comorbidity events", test: (v) => v.events.filter((e) => e.category === "comorbidity").length >= 3 },
      { label: "5+ active medications", test: (v) => (v.polypharmacyCount ?? 0) >= 5 },
      { label: "Ongoing medication course", test: (v) => (overlap.get(v.patientId)?.ongoingCourses ?? 0) > 0 },
    ];
    const rows = factors.map((f) => {
      const withF = views.filter(f.test);
      const withoutF = views.filter((v) => !f.test(v));
      const rWith = rate(withF, (v) => v.escalated === true);
      const rWithout = rate(withoutF, (v) => v.escalated === true);
      const lift = rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : rWith > 0 ? LIFT_CAP : 0;
      return {
        label: f.label,
        n: withF.length,
        nBase: withoutF.length,
        rWith: r2(rWith),
        rWithout: r2(rWithout),
        lift: r2(lift),
      };
    });
    return { rows: rows.sort((a, b) => b.lift - a.lift) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's age, comorbidity events, polypharmacyCount, medication exposure windows, and the modeled escalation outcome event.",
    process: "For each of four fixed factors, splits the cohort into patients with and without it, computes the modeled escalation rate in each arm, and reports lift = rateWith ÷ rateWithout (capped at 99 when the without-rate is zero).",
    output: "rows: one per factor with {label, n, nBase, rWith, rWithout, lift}, sorted worst lift first — how many times higher escalation runs with the factor.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Age, comorbidity count and 5+ medications are verbatim terms of the modeled escalation formula — lift restates the generator.",
      "Escalation is a MODELED outcome event; no factor here is an independent exposure to an observed outcome.",
      "Univariate with/without arms: age, comorbidity and medication burden co-occur, so each lift is confounded by the others.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Every factor is an input to the modeled escalation outcome — lift is circular, not evidence.",
    ],
  },
};
Backward lift

The same lift scan over observed comorbidity events; it returns the full 2×2 table for each candidate so every number is checkable by hand.

 {
  id: "backward-lift",
  name: "Backward lift",
  category: "Discovery & replication",
  description:
    "Per diagnosis category, scans every observed comorbidity and reports the one whose presence lifts the modeled escalation rate the most, returning the full 2×2 for audit.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const rows: Array<{
      category: string; comorbidity: string;
      a: number; b: number; c: number; d: number;
      n: number; rWith: number; rWithout: number; lift: number;
    }> = [];
    for (const [cat, group] of groupBy(views, (v) => v.category ?? "")) {
      const withSets = comorbiditySets(group);
      const allComs = [...new Set(withSets.flatMap((s) => s.coms))].filter((c) => c !== cat);
      let best: (typeof rows)[number] | null = null;
      for (const com of allComs) {
        const withCom = withSets.filter((s) => s.coms.includes(com)).map((s) => s.view);
        const withoutCom = withSets.filter((s) => !s.coms.includes(com)).map((s) => s.view);
        if (withCom.length < 4 || withoutCom.length < 2) continue;
        const a = withCom.filter((v) => v.escalated === true).length;
        const b = withoutCom.filter((v) => v.escalated === true).length;
        const rWith = a / withCom.length;
        const rWithout = b / withoutCom.length;
        const lift = rWithout > 0 ? rWith / rWithout : rWith > 0 ? LIFT_CAP : 0;
        if (!best || lift > best.lift) {
          best = {
            category: cat, comorbidity: com,
            a, b, c: withCom.length - a, d: withoutCom.length - b,
            n: withCom.length, rWith: r2(rWith), rWithout: r2(rWithout), lift: r2(lift),
          };
        }
      }
      if (best) rows.push(best);
    }
    return { rows: rows.sort((x, y) => y.lift - x.lift) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's diagnosis category, comorbidity events, and the modeled escalation outcome event.",
    process: "Within each category, scans every observed comorbidity with at least 4 patients having it and 2 without, computes escalation rate with vs without, and keeps the comorbidity with the highest lift (with ÷ without).",
    output: "rows: one per category with {category, comorbidity, a, b, c, d, n, rWith, rWithout, lift} — the full 2×2 is returned so every lift is auditable.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Escalation is a MODELED outcome event; comorbidity burden is an input of its generating formula — the top lift often restates the injection.",
      "Scanning all observed comorbidities per category and keeping the max is a winner's curse: no multiplicity penalty, extreme lifts win.",
      "Minimum cells of 4 and 2 let tiny strata post lift 99 via the cap sentinel.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Comorbidity is an input to the modeled escalation formula; scan-max then overstates the planted effect.",
    ],
  },
};
Screening → escalation

A patient counts as 'up to date' when their latest screening event falls within the last 365 days; then it compares outcomes of due versus up-to-date patients per category.

 {
  id: "screening-escalation",
  name: "Screening → escalation",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "Are patients behind on screening the ones who escalate? Screening-due versus up-to-date arms (last screening procedure/lab within 365 days) per category, with modeled escalation and readmission rates.",
  requiredCategories: ["procedure", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const upToDate = new Map<string, boolean>();
    for (const v of views) {
      const screens = v.events.filter((e) =>
        (e.category === "procedure" || e.category === "lab") &&
        (e.code.toLowerCase().includes("screen") || e.display.toLowerCase().includes("screen")));
      if (screens.length === 0) { upToDate.set(v.patientId, false); continue; }
      const last = screens[screens.length - 1].t; // events sorted by indexStreams
      const lastT = Date.parse(last);
      const endT = Date.parse(v.events[v.events.length - 1]?.t ?? "");
      upToDate.set(v.patientId,
        Number.isFinite(lastT) && Number.isFinite(endT) &&
        (endT - lastT) / DAY <= SCREENING_RECENCY_DAYS);
    }
    const rows = [...groupBy(views, (v) => v.category ?? "").entries()].map(([cat, g]) => {
      const due = g.filter((v) => upToDate.get(v.patientId) === false);
      const current = g.filter((v) => upToDate.get(v.patientId) === true);
      return {
        label: cat,
        n: g.length,
        nDue: due.length,
        escDue: r2(rate(due, (v) => v.escalated === true)),
        escUptodate: r2(rate(current, (v) => v.escalated === true)),
        readmitDue: r2(rate(due, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true)),
        readmitUptodate: r2(rate(current, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true)),
      };
    });
    return { screeningRecencyDays: SCREENING_RECENCY_DAYS, rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Screening-related procedure and lab events (code or display containing 'screen'), each patient's category, and modeled escalation and readmission outcome events.",
    process: "Marks a patient screening up-to-date when their latest screening event falls within 365 days of their last observed event, splits each category into due vs up-to-date arms, and computes escalation and readmission rates per arm.",
    output: "rows: one per category with {label, n, nDue, escDue, escUptodate, readmitDue, readmitUptodate} — the due-versus-current outcome gap per category.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Escalation and readmission are MODELED outcome events, correlated with the same inputs as screening status in the generator.",
      "Screening status is inferred from 'screen' text matches — a modeling choice, stated here, that can misclassify screening type and recency.",
      "Due/uptodate arms have no minimum n — small-category rates can rest on one or two patients.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "The stratifier and the modeled outcomes are linked inside the generator — the due-vs-current gap is planted.",
    ],
  },
};
Equity check

Compares outcomes across groups of people — sex, age, insurance, region, subgroup — to check whether any group fares noticeably worse than the others.

 {
  id: "equity",
  name: "Equity check",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Modeled escalation and readmission rates across demographic and access dimensions — sex, age band, insurance, and region — to scan whether outcome patterns skew toward a population segment.",
  requiredCategories: ["outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const groupRows = (keyFn: (v: CaseView) => string, withReadmit = false) =>
      [...groupBy(views, keyFn).entries()]
        .map(([k, g]) => ({
          k,
          n: g.length,
          escRate: r2(rate(g, (v) => v.escalated === true)),
          ...(withReadmit ? { readmit: r2(rate(g, (v) => v.readmitted90d === true)) } : {}),
        }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
    const bands: Array<[number, number]> = [[45, 54], [55, 64], [65, 74], [75, 99]];
    const ageRows = bands
      .map(([lo, hi]) => {
        const g = views.filter((v) => (v.age ?? -1) >= lo && (v.age ?? -1) <= hi);
        return { k: hi === 99 ? `${lo}+` : `${lo}–${hi}`, n: g.length, escRate: r2(rate(g, (v) => v.escalated === true)) };
      })
      .filter((r) => r.n > 0);
    return {
      sexRows: groupRows((v) => v.sex ?? ""),
      ageRows,
      insuranceRows: groupRows((v) => v.insurance ?? "", true),
      regionRows: groupRows((v) => v.region ?? ""),
    };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "Each patient's sex, age, insurance, and region from the encounter attributes, plus modeled escalation and readmission outcome events.",
    process: "Groups the cohort separately by sex, age band (45–54 through 75+), insurance, and region, computing the modeled escalation rate in every group — plus the readmission rate for insurance strata.",
    output: "four arrays (sexRows, ageRows, insuranceRows, regionRows) of {k, n, escRate, readmit?} — scan for any segment whose modeled rates skew.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Age band feeds the modeled escalation probability directly — any age skew is injected, not observed disparity.",
      "Escalation and readmission are MODELED outcome events; stratifying them by age is circular by construction.",
      "No race/ethnicity field exists; insurance and region rows are socioeconomic proxies, not protected-class equity analysis.",
      "Rows sort by n, not reliability — single-digit cells sit beside cohort-scale rates with no small-n flag.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Stratifiers either feed the outcome formulas (age) or proxy disadvantage (insurance/region) — treat gaps as generator artifacts.",
    ],
  },
};
Surveillance

Counts admissions per calendar month using each patient's real admission date, and flags the busiest month.

 {
  id: "surveillance",
  name: "Surveillance",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Encounter counts and escalation rates by calendar month of the real admission date, with the peak month flagged — the population-health seasonal scan on the event stream.",
  requiredCategories: ["encounter", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const byMonth = groupBy(views, (v) => String(monthOf(v.events.find((e) => e.category === "encounter")?.t ?? "")));
    const rows = [];
    for (let m = 1; m <= 12; m++) {
      const g = byMonth.get(String(m)) ?? [];
      rows.push({
        month: MONTHS[m - 1],
        n: g.length,
        escRate: rate(g, (v) => v.escalated === true),
      });
    }
    const peak = [...rows].sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n)[0] ?? null;
    return { rows, peak };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream; admission (encounter) dates and outcome events.",
    process: "Groups patients by calendar month of their admission event, counts admissions per month, and computes the escalation rate within each month; flags the busiest month.",
    output: "rows: one per month {month, n, escRate}, plus peak — the seasonal-signal view; months with no admissions show n = 0.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Counts ride real admission dates, but admission month was partly modeled at import — month clustering may reflect the importer, not epidemiology.",
      "Monthly escRate stratifies a modeled outcome on small month slices — small-n noise, not signal.",
    ],
  },
};
Seasonal pattern

Uses each admission's real date to place patients in seasons, then reports how the condition mix and escalation rate shift with the calendar.

 {
  id: "seasonal-pattern",
  name: "Seasonal pattern",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Category mix and escalation rate by season (winter = Dec–Feb) derived from each admission's real date — whether cohort composition shifts with the calendar.",
  requiredCategories: ["encounter", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const seasons = ["Winter", "Spring", "Summer", "Fall"];
    const rows = seasons.map((s) => {
      const g = views.filter((v) => seasonOf(monthOf(v.events.find((e) => e.category === "encounter")?.t ?? "")) === s);
      const byCat = groupBy(g, (v) => v.category ?? "(unknown)");
      const mix = [...byCat.entries()]
        .map(([cat, grp]) => ({ cat, share: g.length ? grp.length / g.length : 0 }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.share - a.share);
      return {
        label: s,
        n: g.length,
        escRate: rate(g, (v) => v.escalated === true),
        topCategory: mix[0]?.cat ?? "—",
        topShare: mix[0]?.share ?? 0,
      };
    });
    return { rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: admission dates, encounter attributes (category), outcome events.",
    process: "Derives each patient's season from the admission event's month (winter = Dec–Feb), then per season counts patients, computes escalation rate, and reports the most common category and its share.",
    output: "rows: one per season {label, n, escRate, topCategory, topShare} — whether composition shifts with the calendar.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Admission dates are real but admission month was partly modeled at import — season gradients may be import artifacts.",
      "topCategory share in small seasons flips with a handful of patients.",
      "Seasonal escRate re-stratifies a modeled outcome.",
    ],
  },
};
Time-to-escalation

Measures days from each patient's real admission date to their escalation outcome event, grouped by category and illness burden.

 {
  id: "time-to-escalation",
  name: "Time-to-escalation",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Days from each escalated patient's admission date to the escalation outcome event — by category and by comorbidity burden (1 through 5+ conditions).",
  requiredCategories: ["encounter", "outcome", "comorbidity"],
  run(events) {
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const rowsFor = (groups: Map<string, typeof views>) =>
      [...groups.entries()]
        .map(([key, g]) => {
          const times = g
            .filter((v) => v.escalated)
            .map((v) => {
              const admit = v.events.find((e) => e.category === "encounter");
              const esc = v.events.find((e) => e.category === "outcome" && e.code === "escalation");
              return admit && esc ? daysBetween(admit.t, esc.t) : NaN;
            })
            .filter((x) => Number.isFinite(x));
          return { key, n: times.length, median: median(times), mean: mean(times, (x) => x) };
        })
        .sort((a, b) => (a.median ?? Infinity) - (b.median ?? Infinity));
    const esc = views.filter((v) => v.escalated);
    const byCat = groupBy(esc, (v) => v.category ?? "(unknown)");
    const byCom = groupBy(esc, (v) => {
      const n = v.events.filter((e) => e.category === "comorbidity").length;
      return `${Math.min(n, 5)}${n >= 5 ? "+" : ""} comorb.`;
    });
    return {
      n: esc.length,
      byCat: rowsFor(byCat),
      byCom: rowsFor(byCom).sort((a, b) => Number(a.key.split(" ")[0]) - Number(b.key.split(" ")[0])),
    };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: admission dates, escalation outcome events, comorbidity events, encounter attributes.",
    process: "Filters to escalated patients, measures days from each admission date to the escalation outcome date, then groups by category and by comorbidity-burden band and reports median and mean days.",
    output: "n plus byCat and byCom rows {key, n, median, mean} — how quickly care escalates, by clinical context.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "The interval uses real dates, but the escalation endpoint itself is a modeled outcome event — medians restate the generator's ranges.",
      "The comorbidity-burden axis is an input to the modeled escalation — circular comparison.",
      "Conditioned on escalated patients only — never-escalated patients are invisible to every median.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Endpoint and its stratifier are both products of the import model.",
    ],
  },
};
Kaplan–Meier curve

A real Kaplan–Meier over actual dates: the clock starts at each patient's first event, the 'event' is the first appearance of a target concept you choose, and patients without it are followed until their last event.

 {
  id: "km-curve",
  name: "Kaplan–Meier curve",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Kaplan–Meier time-to-event over OBSERVED event dates: time from each patient's first event to the first occurrence of a target concept, censored at the patient's last event. Param-driven target; defaults to the most common diagnosis.",
  requiredCategories: ["diagnosis"],
  run(events, params) {
    const cat = (params?.category as EventCategory) ?? "diagnosis";
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const streams = views.map((v) => v.events).filter((s) => s.length > 0);
    // Default target: the most common concept in the chosen category (deterministic).
    let match = params?.target ?? "";
    if (!match) {
      const idx = conceptIndex(events, cat);
      let bestN = 0;
      for (const [key, ci] of idx) {
        if (ci.patients.size > bestN) { bestN = ci.patients.size; match = key; }
      }
    }
    if (!match) return { n: 0, target: null, steps: [], medianDay: null, ready: false };
    const steps = kmCurve(streams, cat, match);
    const medianDay = steps.find((s) => s.survival <= 0.5)?.day ?? null;
    return { n: streams.length, target: match, category: cat, steps, medianDay };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream; params category (default 'diagnosis') and target (code or display substring; default = most common concept in the category).",
    process: "Runs a real Kaplan–Meier over observed dates: time zero is each patient's first event, the event is the first occurrence of the target concept, patients without it are censored at their last event.",
    output: "n, target, category, steps {day, survival, atRisk, events}, medianDay — a dropping curve means the target concept occurring as follow-up accumulates.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Real time-to-event from observed dates over an observed concept — but 'survival' is time to first DOCUMENTATION, which conflates onset with recording.",
      "Censoring at last recorded event treats documentation gaps as observation time.",
      "If the target is the modeled 'escalation' outcome, this becomes serious: event timing is modeled, so the curve traces the generator.",
    ],
  },
};
Transition matrix

Splits each patient's timeline into episodes separated by quiet gaps, names each episode by its main concept, and computes the probability of moving from one episode type to the next.

 {
  id: "transition-matrix",
  name: "Transition matrix",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "Markov-style episode-to-episode transitions: episodes are gap-separated spans of each patient's event stream; each episode's state is its leading concept, and the matrix counts every observed state→next-state hop.",
  requiredCategories: ["diagnosis"],
  run(events, params) {
    const gapDays = Math.max(1, Number(params?.gapDays ?? 30));
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const transitions = new Map<string, number>();
    const fromTotals = new Map<string, number>();
    let patients = 0;
    for (const v of views) {
      const states = episodeStates(v, gapDays);
      if (states.length < 2) continue;
      patients++;
      for (let i = 0; i < states.length - 1; i++) {
        const k = `${states[i]}|${states[i + 1]}`;
        transitions.set(k, (transitions.get(k) ?? 0) + 1);
        fromTotals.set(states[i], (fromTotals.get(states[i]) ?? 0) + 1);
      }
    }
    const rows = [...fromTotals.entries()]
      .map(([from, total]) => {
        const dist = [...transitions.entries()]
          .filter(([k]) => k.startsWith(`${from}|`))
          .map(([k, n]) => ({ to: k.split("|")[1], n, prob: total ? n / total : 0 }))
          .sort((a, b) => b.prob - a.prob || b.n - a.n);
        return { from, n: total, dist, top: dist[0] ? { to: dist[0].to, prob: dist[0].prob } : null };
      })
      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
    const totalN = rows.reduce((a, r) => a + r.n, 0);
    const weightedTopProb = totalN
      ? rows.reduce((a, r) => a + (r.top?.prob ?? 0) * r.n, 0) / totalN
      : 0;
    return { gapDays, patients, rows: topN(rows, 15), weightedTopProb };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream; param gapDays (default 30) for the episode boundary.",
    process: "Splits each patient's stream into gap-separated episodes, takes each episode's leading concept as its state, then counts every observed state→next-state hop across patients and converts counts to probabilities.",
    output: "gapDays, patients, rows {from, n, dist [{to, n, prob}], top}, weightedTopProb — near-1 weightedTopProb means a near-deterministic hop.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Episode boundaries are a gap heuristic — the gapDays choice reshapes the matrix; sensitivity is unmeasured.",
      "State = leading concept per episode; within-episode richness is discarded.",
      "Rows with tiny n still report probabilities — weightedTopProb smooths over unstable hops.",
    ],
  },
};
Trajectory map

The same flow diagram, but the stages are a patient's successive care episodes (gap-separated spans of their event stream) rather than fixed checkpoints.

 {
  id: "trajectory-map",
  name: "Trajectory map",
  category: "Overview",
  description:
    "Patients walked through successive care episodes: each stage is an episode ordinal with its state distribution, every ribbon one group's transition between consecutive episodes; optional single-patient overlay.",
  requiredCategories: ["diagnosis"],
  run(events, params) {
    const gapDays = Math.max(1, Number(params?.gapDays ?? 30));
    const maxStage = Math.max(2, Math.min(Number(params?.maxStages ?? 6), 10));
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const stageStates = new Map<number, Map<string, number>>();
    const flows = new Map<number, Map<string, number>>();
    const reached = new Map<number, number>();
    const highlightId = params?.patientId ?? null;
    let highlight: string[] | null = null;
    for (const v of views) {
      const states = episodeStates(v, gapDays).slice(0, maxStage);
      if (highlightId && v.patientId === highlightId) highlight = states;
      for (let i = 0; i < states.length; i++) {
        const sm = stageStates.get(i) ?? new Map<string, number>();
        sm.set(states[i], (sm.get(states[i]) ?? 0) + 1);
        stageStates.set(i, sm);
        reached.set(i, (reached.get(i) ?? 0) + 1);
        if (i > 0) {
          const fm = flows.get(i) ?? new Map<string, number>();
          const k = `${states[i - 1]}|${states[i]}`;
          fm.set(k, (fm.get(k) ?? 0) + 1);
          flows.set(i, fm);
        }
      }
    }
    const stages = [...stageStates.keys()].sort((a, b) => a - b).map((i) => ({
      label: `Episode ${i + 1}`,
      reached: reached.get(i) ?? 0,
      rows: [...(stageStates.get(i) ?? new Map<string, number>())]
        .map(([value, n]) => ({ value, n, pct: (reached.get(i) ?? 0) ? n / (reached.get(i) ?? 1) : 0 }))
        .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n),
    }));
    const edges = [...flows.keys()].sort((a, b) => a - b).map((i) => ({
      from: `Episode ${i}`,
      to: `Episode ${i + 1}`,
      flows: [...(flows.get(i) ?? new Map<string, number>())]
        .map(([k, n]) => { const [a, b] = k.split("|"); return { a, b, n }; })
        .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n),
    }));
    return { gapDays, stages, edges, highlight, highlightCaseId: highlightId, total: views.length };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream; params gapDays, maxStages, patientId (optional overlay).",
    process: "Builds gap-separated episodes per patient, takes each episode's leading concept as its state, then reports the state distribution per episode ordinal and every observed transition between consecutive episodes; optionally overlays one patient's path.",
    output: "stages [{label, reached, rows:[{value, n, pct}]}], edges per-hop flows {a, b, n}, highlight, total — ribbons show how states evolve across episodes.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Episode ordinals are a gap-heuristic construction, not checked clinical stages — gapDays reshapes the map.",
      "State = leading concept per episode; transitions are documentation sequences, not verified care flow.",
      "Sparse later episodes thin out fast — tail stages rest on few patients.",
    ],
  },
};
Co-occurrence

Now uses real dates: for each pair of conditions it reports which one was documented first more often (ties shown separately) instead of guessing alphabetically.

 {
  id: "cooccurrence",
  name: "Co-occurrence",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Comorbidity pairs co-occurring in a patient, with the shared escalation rate and — now with real dates — which condition was documented first more often.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const perPatient = firstByPatientConcept(events, "comorbidity");
    const esc = escPatientsOf(events, "escalation");
    const pairs = new Map<string, { a: string; b: string; n: number; bothEsc: number; aFirst: number; tie: number }>();
    for (const [pid, concepts] of perPatient) {
      const entries = [...concepts.values()];
      for (let i = 0; i < entries.length; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < entries.length; j++) {
          const [a, b, key] = pairKey(entries[i].display, entries[j].display);
          let rec = pairs.get(key);
          if (!rec) { rec = { a, b, n: 0, bothEsc: 0, aFirst: 0, tie: 0 }; pairs.set(key, rec); }
          rec.n++;
          if (!esc.has(pid)) continue;
          rec.bothEsc++;
          const firstA = (entries[i].display === a ? entries[i] : entries[j]).first;
          const firstB = (entries[i].display === a ? entries[j] : entries[i]).first;
          if (firstA < firstB) rec.aFirst++;
          else if (firstA === firstB) rec.tie++;
        }
      }
    }
    const rows = [...pairs.values()]
      .map((r) => ({
        a: r.a, b: r.b, n: r.n,
        bothEscRate: r.n ? r.bothEsc / r.n : 0,
        aFirstRate: r.bothEsc - r.tie > 0 ? r.aFirst / (r.bothEsc - r.tie) : 0,
        tieRate: r.bothEsc > 0 ? r.tie / r.bothEsc : 0,
        sequence: r.bothEsc > 0 ? `both escalated in ${r.bothEsc} patients` : "no shared escalations",
      }))
      .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n);
    return { rows: topN(rows, 10) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: comorbidity events with dates, escalation outcome events.",
    process: "Tallies every comorbidity pair co-occurring within a patient, computes the shared-escalation rate, and uses each condition's first documented date to see which came first more often (ties reported separately).",
    output: "rows: top 10 pairs {a, b, n, bothEscRate, aFirstRate, tieRate, sequence} — ordering is observed documentation order, not a proxy.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "bothEscRate is circular: comorbidities feed the modeled escalation outcome.",
      "Uncoded comorbidity labels anchor at the admit date — first-documented order degenerates into ties for those.",
      "Pair counts share patients — n overstates precision.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Pair escalation rates restate the generator's comorbidity→escalation formula.",
    ],
  },
};
Symptom co-occurrence

Finds which pairs of symptoms get documented together most often, regardless of diagnosis.

 {
  id: "symptom-cooc",
  name: "Symptom co-occurrence",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "The most frequently co-documented symptom pairs, with the escalation rate among patients carrying each pair — which symptoms travel together regardless of diagnosis.",
  requiredCategories: ["symptom", "outcome"],
  run(events) {
    const sets = conceptSets(events, "symptom");
    const esc = escPatientsOf(events, "escalation");
    const pairs = new Map<string, { a: string; b: string; n: number; esc: number }>();
    for (const [pid, s] of sets) {
      const items = [...s].sort();
      for (let i = 0; i < items.length; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < items.length; j++) {
          const key = `${items[i]} + ${items[j]}`;
          let rec = pairs.get(key);
          if (!rec) { rec = { a: items[i], b: items[j], n: 0, esc: 0 }; pairs.set(key, rec); }
          rec.n++;
          if (esc.has(pid)) rec.esc++;
        }
      }
    }
    const rows = [...pairs.values()]
      .filter((r) => r.n >= 4)
      .sort((x, y) => y.n - x.n)
      .map((r) => ({ label: `${r.a} + ${r.b}`, n: r.n, escRate: r.esc / r.n }));
    return { rows: topN(rows, 10) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: symptom events, escalation outcome events.",
    process: "Tallies every co-documented symptom pair within a patient using concept keys, keeps pairs in at least 4 patients, and computes the escalation rate among patients carrying each pair; top 10 by volume.",
    output: "rows: {label ('A + B'), n, escRate} — which symptoms travel together.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Pair membership rides documentation; uncoded symptoms anchor at the admit date but do not distort pair membership.",
      "escRate attached to pairs is a modeled outcome correlated with comorbidity burden, not with symptoms.",
      "Patients count once per pair carried — pair ns overlap.",
    ],
  },
};
Symptom burden

Checks whether patients with more distinct conditions also document more distinct symptoms, using concept counts from the stream.

 {
  id: "symptom-burden",
  name: "Symptom burden",
  category: "Patient / evidence",
  description:
    "Average documented symptom count by comorbidity burden (1 through 5+ distinct conditions) — whether sicker patients present with more documented symptoms.",
  requiredCategories: ["symptom", "comorbidity", "medication"],
  run(events) {
    const comSets = conceptSets(events, "comorbidity");
    const symSets = conceptSets(events, "symptom");
    const poly = polyCounts(events);
    const medEvents = eventCountMap(events, "medication");
    const bands = new Map<string, { n: number; sym: number[]; meds: number[] }>();
    for (const pid of patientIds(events)) {
      const nCom = comSets.get(pid)?.size ?? 0;
      const band = `${Math.min(nCom, 5)}${nCom >= 5 ? "+" : ""}`;
      const rec = bands.get(band) ?? { n: 0, sym: [], meds: [] };
      rec.n++;
      rec.sym.push(symSets.get(pid)?.size ?? 0);
      const p = poly.get(pid);
      rec.meds.push(typeof p === "number" ? p : medEvents.get(pid) ?? 0);
      bands.set(band, rec);
    }
    const rows = [...bands.entries()]
      .map(([com, r]) => ({ com, n: r.n, avgSymptoms: mean(r.sym, (x) => x), avgMeds: mean(r.meds, (x) => x) }))
      .sort((a, b) => Number(a.com.split(" ")[0]) - Number(b.com.split(" ")[0]));
    return { rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: symptom, comorbidity, and medication events; encounter attributes (polypharmacyCount).",
    process: "Bands patients by their count of DISTINCT documented comorbidity concepts (1 through 5+), then averages distinct symptom count and active medication count within each band.",
    output: "rows: {com, n, avgSymptoms, avgMeds} — whether sicker patients carry more documented symptoms.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Documented counts, not lived burden: coding intensity drives both axes.",
      "avgMeds invites a polypharmacy reading the generator injects independently of symptoms.",
      "Patients with zero documented comorbidities land in the '0' band — read any gradient against that floor.",
    ],
  },
};
Symptom → investigation

Checks whether a lab actually happened within a set number of days after a symptom's first date — a timing-aware missed-work-up scan.

 {
  id: "symptom-investigation",
  name: "Symptom → investigation",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "For each common symptom, how often any lab work-up is documented on/after the symptom's first date within a window — the missed-work-up scan on dated events.",
  requiredCategories: ["symptom", "lab"],
  run(events, params) {
    const windowDays = Math.max(0, Number(params?.windowDays ?? 30));
    const minN = Math.max(1, Number(params?.minN ?? 15));
    const symFirst = firstByPatientConcept(events, "symptom");
    const rows: Array<{ label: string; n: number; investigatedRate: number }> = [];
    for (const [concept, m] of symFirst) {
      if (m.size < minN) continue;
      let investigated = 0;
      let n = 0;
      for (const [pid, info] of m) {
        n++;
        const due = Date.parse(info.first) + windowDays * DAY;
        const hit = events.some((e) =>
          e.patientId === pid && e.category === "lab" &&
          Date.parse(e.t) >= Date.parse(info.first) && Date.parse(e.t) <= due);
        if (hit) investigated++;
      }
      rows.push({ label: [...m.values()][0].display || concept, n, investigatedRate: n ? investigated / n : 0 });
    }
    return { windowDays, rows: rows.sort((a, b) => a.investigatedRate - b.investigatedRate) };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: symptom and lab events; params windowDays (default 30), minN (default 15).",
    process: "For each symptom seen in at least minN patients, computes the share with at least one lab event dated between the symptom's first date and windowDays later; sorted lowest rate first.",
    output: "windowDays plus rows {label, n, investigatedRate} — the missed-work-up scan: symptoms whose work-ups often never get ordered in the window.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Symptoms documented without a date anchor at the admit date — the window can be zero-width or negative for them.",
      "'Any lab at all' is a crude work-up proxy credited to every symptom the patient has.",
      "Symptoms under minN vanish silently; no expected-work-up denominator per symptom.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Anchored dates plus any-lab attribution make the 'missed work-up' reading unsafe for fallback-coded cohorts.",
    ],
  },
};
Phenotype clusters

The same no-randomness clustering, but over patients' coded condition profiles built from their event streams.

 {
  id: "phenotype-clusters",
  name: "Phenotype clusters",
  category: "Clinical / diagnostic",
  description:
    "Deterministic k-means over patients' comorbidity-concept profiles: groups patients by their co-occurring bundle of conditions rather than their primary category. Seeded without randomness.",
  requiredCategories: ["comorbidity"],
  run(events, params) {
    const k = Math.min(Math.max(Number(params?.k ?? 4), 2), 6);
    const comSets = conceptSets(events, "comorbidity");
    const symSets = conceptSets(events, "symptom");
    const poly = polyCounts(events);
    const esc = escPatientsOf(events, "escalation");
    const readmit = escPatientsOf(events, "readmission-90d");
    const vocab = [...conceptIndex(events, "comorbidity").keys()].sort();
    const pids = [...comSets.keys()].filter((p) => (comSets.get(p)?.size ?? 0) > 0).sort();
    if (pids.length < k * 6) return { k, n: pids.length, clusters: [], ready: false };
    const vectors: number[][] = pids.map((p) => vocab.map((c) => (comSets.get(p)?.has(c) ? 1 : 0)));
    const dist2 = (a: number[], b: number[]) => a.reduce((s, v, i) => s + (v - b[i]) * (v - b[i]), 0);
    const order = pids
      .map((_, i) => i)
      .sort((a, b) => comSets.get(pids[a])!.size - comSets.get(pids[b])!.size || pids[a].localeCompare(pids[b]));
    let centroids = Array.from({ length: k }, (_, i) => vectors[order[Math.floor((i * order.length) / k)]].slice());
    let assign = new Array(vectors.length).fill(0);
    for (let iter = 0; iter < 30; iter++) {
      let changed = false;
      vectors.forEach((v, i) => {
        let best = 0, bestD = Infinity;
        centroids.forEach((c, ci) => {
          const d = dist2(v, c);
          if (d < bestD) { bestD = d; best = ci; }
        });
        if (assign[i] !== best) { assign[i] = best; changed = true; }
      });
      centroids = centroids.map((c, ci) => {
        const members = vectors.filter((_, i) => assign[i] === ci);
        return members.length ? c.map((_, d) => mean(members.map((m) => m[d]), (x) => x)) : c;
      });
      if (!changed) break;
    }
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const viewOf = new Map(views.map((v) => [v.patientId, v]));
    const clusters = centroids
      .map((_, ci) => {
        const members = pids.filter((_, i) => assign[i] === ci);
        if (!members.length) return null;
        const profile = vocab.map((name) => ({
          name,
          rate: members.filter((p) => comSets.get(p)?.has(name)).length / members.length,
        })).sort((a, b) => b.rate - a.rate);
        const hallmark = profile.filter((p) => p.rate >= 0.35).slice(0, 3);
        const byCat = groupBy(members.map((p) => viewOf.get(p)!), (v) => v.category ?? "(unknown)");
        const dominant = [...byCat.entries()].sort((a, b) => b[1].length - a[1].length)[0];
        return {
          id: ci,
          n: members.length,
          hallmark: hallmark.length ? hallmark : profile.slice(0, 1),
          avgSymptoms: mean(members.map((p) => symSets.get(p)?.size ?? 0), (x) => x),
          avgComCount: mean(members.map((p) => comSets.get(p)?.size ?? 0), (x) => x),
          escRate: members.filter((p) => esc.has(p)).length / members.length,
          readmitRate: members.filter((p) => readmit.has(p)).length / members.length,
          avgLos: mean(members.map((p) => viewOf.get(p)?.losDays ?? NaN), (x) => x),
          dominantCategory: dominant?.[0] ?? "(unknown)",
          dominantCategoryShare: members.length ? (dominant?.[1].length ?? 0) / members.length : 0,
        };
      })
      .filter((c): c is NonNullable<typeof c> => c !== null)
      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n);
    return { k, n: pids.length, clusters, ready: true };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: distinct comorbidity concepts per patient as a multi-hot vector; param k (default 4, clamped 2–6).",
    process: "Runs deterministic k-means (up to 30 iterations, seeded from evenly-spaced points along a comorbidity-count sort — never randomly) so the same stream always yields the same clusters; profiles each cluster's hallmark conditions and outcomes.",
    output: "k, n, ready, clusters {id, n, hallmark:[{name, rate}], avgSymptoms, avgComCount, escRate, readmitRate, avgLos, dominantCategory, dominantCategoryShare}.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "serious",
    points: [
      "Cluster escRate/readmitRate are modeled from comorbidity burden — clusters re-display the injection, not discovered risk.",
      "Centroids seed along a comorbidity-count sort — ordering is biased toward the burden gradient.",
      "Hallmark at rate ≥0.35 can rest on a handful of members.",
    ],
    serious: [
      "Cluster outcomes are formulas of the clustering variable.",
    ],
  },
};
Screening coverage

Uses each patient's dated screening events to label them up to date, overdue, or never screened, then aggregates by category.

 {
  id: "screening",
  name: "Screening coverage",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Screening-procedure coverage from dated events: patients up to date, overdue, or never screened — by category, the care-gap view at population scale.",
  requiredCategories: ["procedure", "encounter"],
  run(events, params) {
    const match = params?.match ?? "screening";
    const withinDays = Math.max(1, Number(params?.withinDays ?? 365));
    const asOf = params?.asOf || latestDate(events);
    if (!asOf) return { asOf: null, rows: [], ready: false };
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const byPatient = new Map(views.map((v) => [v.patientId, v.events]));
    const gaps = gapAnalysis(byPatient, "procedure", match, asOf, withinDays);
    const rows = [...groupBy(views, (v) => v.category ?? "(unknown)").entries()].map(([cat, g]) => {
      const pids = new Set(g.map((v) => v.patientId));
      const up = gaps.upToDate.filter((p) => pids.has(p)).length;
      const od = gaps.overdue.filter((r) => pids.has(r.patientId)).length;
      const nv = gaps.never.filter((p) => pids.has(p)).length;
      const denom = up + od + nv;
      return {
        cat, n: g.length,
        upToDateRate: denom ? up / denom : 0,
        overdue: od,
        never: nv,
        gaps: od + nv,
      };
    }).sort((a, b) => a.upToDateRate - b.upToDateRate);
    return { asOf, withinDays, match, rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: dated procedure events; params match (default 'screening'), withinDays (default 365), asOf (default = latest event date).",
    process: "Runs gap analysis on the matched procedure per patient — last occurrence within withinDays of asOf is up to date, older is overdue, none is never — then aggregates by category.",
    output: "asOf, match, rows per category {cat, n, upToDateRate, overdue, never, gaps} — worst coverage first.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Coverage is measured against documented procedure events — under-documentation reads as a gap.",
      "asOf defaults to the latest event in the stream; patients near the stream's edge look artificially current.",
      "Uncoded procedures anchor at the admit date — timing is only as good as the coding.",
    ],
  },
};
Vaccination status

Applies the same dated gap logic to vaccination events: current, overdue, or never, per category.

 {
  id: "vaccination",
  name: "Vaccination status",
  category: "Public health / population",
  description:
    "Vaccination coverage from dated events: patients vaccinated within the window, past-due, or never — by category, from vaccination procedure events.",
  requiredCategories: ["procedure", "encounter"],
  run(events, params) {
    const match = params?.match ?? "vaccin";
    const withinDays = Math.max(1, Number(params?.withinDays ?? 365));
    const asOf = params?.asOf || latestDate(events);
    if (!asOf) return { asOf: null, rows: [], ready: false };
    const views = indexStreams(events);
    const byPatient = new Map(views.map((v) => [v.patientId, v.events]));
    const gaps = gapAnalysis(byPatient, "procedure", match, asOf, withinDays);
    const everVax = new Set([...gaps.upToDate, ...gaps.overdue.map((r) => r.patientId)]);
    const rows = [...groupBy(views, (v) => v.category ?? "(unknown)").entries()].map(([cat, g]) => {
      const pids = g.map((v) => v.patientId);

      return {
        label: cat,
        n: g.length,
        coverage: pids.length ? vax.length / pids.length : 0,
        current: pids.filter((p) => gaps.upToDate.includes(p)).length,
        overdue: gaps.overdue.filter((r) => pids.includes(r.patientId)).length,
        never: gaps.never.filter((p) => pids.includes(p)).length,
      };
    }).sort((a, b) => a.coverage - b.coverage);
    return { asOf, withinDays, match, rows };
  },
  howTo: {
    input: "The cohort's event stream: dated procedure events; params match (default 'vaccin'), withinDays (default 365), asOf (default = latest event date).",
    process: "Runs gap analysis for vaccination procedures per patient: a recorded vaccination within withinDays is current, an older one is overdue, none is never; aggregates coverage per category.",
    output: "asOf, match, rows per category {label, n, coverage, current, overdue, never} — lowest coverage first.",
  },
  audit: {
    verdict: "caution",
    points: [
      "Coverage rests on documented vaccination procedure events; unvaccinated and unrecorded are indistinguishable in 'never'.",
      "Uncoded procedures anchor at the admit date — 'current' can be an import artifact.",
      "Descriptive coverage only — no outcome contrast is computed, by design.",
    ],
  },
